Tesla BLE KMP User Guide

From installation to key enrollment, state queries and every control API. Instructions and examples for app developers in one document.

M6 stage 1 (iOS Swift package) · Android 12+ · iOS 16+
Styles and scripts are embedded for offline use. Switching languages requires the other language files.

Installation and project setup

The iOS library is published as a Swift package from version 1.0.0. Android and JVM are not published yet (Maven Central comes later): build them from a source checkout and do not invent Maven coordinates. CocoaPods is not supported. Dependency downloads need Internet access during a build; vehicle communication uses BLE only.

iOS: Swift Package Manager

In Xcode choose File > Add Package Dependencies, enter the URL below, choose the dependency rule Up to Next Major Version from 1.0.0, and add the Teslable product to the app target. In a Package.swift, add the package to dependencies and the product to the dependencies of your target.

.package(url: "https://github.com/smallmiro/tesla-ble-kmp", from: "1.0.0")
.product(name: "Teslable", package: "tesla-ble-kmp")

Requirements: iOS 16 or later and NSBluetoothAlwaysUsageDescription in Info.plist. The framework is static, with an arm64 device slice and an arm64 simulator slice only: use the simulator on an Apple Silicon Mac, and set EXCLUDED_ARCHS[sdk=iphonesimulator*] = x86_64 for simulator builds of every architecture (for example Release). The simulator has no Bluetooth, so scanning and connecting need a real iPhone, and its Keychain needs a signed app (ad-hoc signing is enough). Details are in Platform notes and Troubleshooting.

Then import Teslable. Swift gets no Kotlin default-argument overloads, so pass every argument, including timeout: nil. Use withSeconds and Timeouts for time limits. Full Swift examples are in Getting started and Platform notes. The configuration below does not log.

import Teslable
let ble = TeslaBle.companion.create(
    platform: PlatformContext(),
    config: TeslaBleConfig.companion.default(logger: TeslaLoggerCompanion.shared.NoOp)
)

Android and JVM: build from source

Development is complete, but the Android real-device check is not finished, so there is no Maven artifact yet. Use Git, a JDK 17 toolchain (provisioned by Gradle), Android SDK 37 and an Android API 31+ device, with the repository Gradle Wrapper and pinned dependency versions. The verified path is an app module depending on :sdk within the complete source Gradle build. Build the Android sample first, then add your app module to that same build and add this dependency. Copying only :sdk omits required internal modules and build plugins.

git clone https://github.com/smallmiro/tesla-ble-kmp
cd tesla-ble-kmp
./gradlew :sdk:assemble
./gradlew :samples:android:assembleDebug
dependencies {
    implementation(project(":sdk"))
}

Set app minSdk to at least 31. Declare and request BLUETOOTH_SCAN and BLUETOOTH_CONNECT before scanning. Exclude key files from backup. Exact XML and storage settings are in Platform notes. The default configuration below does not log.

val ble = TeslaBle.create(PlatformContext(context), TeslaBleConfig())

iOS framework from source

To change the library or run the sample app, build the framework from source instead of using the package. Link the iosArm64 static framework for a device, and the iosSimulatorArm64 framework for an Apple Silicon simulator. Add the matching output directory to Framework Search Paths and link Teslable.framework with Do Not Embed (it is static). Rebuild the framework when Kotlin code changes. The existing XcodeGen sample shows both configurations.

./gradlew :sdk:linkDebugFrameworkIosSimulatorArm64
./gradlew :sdk:linkDebugFrameworkIosArm64
sdk/build/bin/iosArm64/debugFramework/Teslable.framework
sdk/build/bin/iosSimulatorArm64/debugFramework/Teslable.framework

Method-by-method API reference

Scope: the public APIs and input/result models an app uses through :sdk.

These are individual call examples. ble, vehicle, key and vin come from the getting-started flow; inputs are illustrative. Each call is independent, not a script to execute in sequence. Run suspend calls in a coroutine. Imports: dev.mirolab.teslable.*, dev.mirolab.teslable.model.*, dev.mirolab.teslable.port.*, kotlin.time.Duration.Companion.seconds. Schedule types are in com.tesla.generated.carserver.common.*.

Vehicle.startSession()

public suspend fun startSession( domains: Set<VehicleDomain> = VehicleDomain.ALL, timeout: Duration? = null): VehicleResult<Unit>
ParameterType / defaultMeaning, units and accepted values
domainsSet<VehicleDomain>
VehicleDomain.ALL		Domains to establish. ALL contains VCSEC and INFOTAINMENT.
timeoutDuration?
null		Whole-call time limit. null uses the config default: commands 5 s, handshake 20 s, connect 30 s, pairing 60 s.

Call example

val result = vehicle.startSession(domains = setOf(VehicleDomain.VCSEC, VehicleDomain.INFOTAINMENT), timeout = 10.seconds)

Return value

VehicleResult<Unit>

Success(Unit): requested domains are ready for authentication, including cache restores. Failure(error): RequiresKey/KeyNotPaired/Locked/Timeout, etc. Uses handshakeTimeout by default.

Feature details and call order · Returned fields and error handling

Vehicle.disconnect()

public suspend fun disconnect(): Unit

No parameters

Call example

vehicle.disconnect()

Return value

Unit

Unit. Idempotent cleanup: save cache, wipe session keys, release links. close cleans up all connections; a closed TeslaBle cannot be reused.

Feature details and call order · Returned fields and error handling

Vehicle.vehicleStatus()

public suspend fun vehicleStatus(timeout: Duration? = null): VehicleResult<VehicleStatus>

No authenticated session required. Connect over BLE first.

ParameterType / defaultMeaning, units and accepted values
timeoutDuration?
null		Whole-call time limit. null uses the config default: commands 5 s, handshake 20 s, connect 30 s, pairing 60 s.

Call example

val result = vehicle.vehicleStatus(timeout = 10.seconds)

Return value

VehicleResult<VehicleStatus>

Success(value: VehicleStatus). lockState, closures, sleepStatus, userPresence, tonneauPercentOpen, gear. LOCKED/ASLEEP/PARK are illustrative, not a promised response. Uncertain(error) / Failure(error).

Feature details and call order · Returned fields and error handling

Vehicle.getState()

public suspend fun getState( category: StateCategory, timeout: Duration? = null): VehicleResult<VehicleData>

Requires an INFOTAINMENT session and an awake vehicle. Call after startSession(setOf(VehicleDomain.INFOTAINMENT)) succeeds. Does not start a session or wake the vehicle automatically.

ParameterType / defaultMeaning, units and accepted values
categoryStateCategory
required		One category per request. All 12 values and fields are in Reading state.
timeoutDuration?
null		Whole-call time limit. null uses the config default: commands 5 s, handshake 20 s, connect 30 s, pairing 60 s.

Call example

val result = vehicle.getState(category = StateCategory.CHARGE, timeout = 10.seconds)

Return value

VehicleResult<VehicleData>

Success(value: VehicleData). Nullable state messages for category; e.g. charge_state?.battery_level=80 (illustrative). Missing=null, not zero. Empty VehicleData can also succeed. Uncertain(error) / Failure(error).

Feature details and call order · Returned fields and error handling

public suspend fun keySummary(timeout: Duration? = null): VehicleResult<WhitelistSummary>

No authenticated session required. Connect over BLE first.

Call example

Return value

VehicleResult<WhitelistSummary>

Feature details and call order · Returned fields and error handling

public suspend fun listKeys(timeout: Duration? = null): VehicleResult<List<WhitelistEntry>>

No authenticated session required. Connect over BLE first.

null		Whole-call time limit. null uses the config default: commands 5 s, handshake 20 s, connect 30 s, pairing 60 s.

Call example

Return value

VehicleResult<List<WhitelistEntry>>

Success(value: List<WhitelistEntry>). List of slot, publicKey?, keyIdSha1Hex?, role and formFactor per key; timeout covers the whole list. Uncertain(error) / Failure(error).

Feature details and call order · Returned fields and error handling

Vehicle.sessionInfo()

public suspend fun sessionInfo( publicKey: PublicKeyBytes, domain: VehicleDomain, timeout: Duration? = null): VehicleResult<SessionInfoSummary>

No authenticated session required. Connect over BLE first.

ParameterType / defaultMeaning, units and accepted values
publicKeyPublicKeyBytes
required		65-byte uncompressed P-256 public key: 0x04 || X(32) || Y(32).
domainVehicleDomain
required		VCSEC or INFOTAINMENT. Verify enrollment using INFOTAINMENT.
timeoutDuration?
null		Whole-call time limit. null uses the config default: commands 5 s, handshake 20 s, connect 30 s, pairing 60 s.

Call example

val result = vehicle.sessionInfo(publicKey = key.publicKey, domain = VehicleDomain.INFOTAINMENT, timeout = 10.seconds)

Return value

VehicleResult<SessionInfoSummary>

Success(value: SessionInfoSummary). counter(UInt), clockTime(UInt, seconds since epoch start), vehiclePublicKey?. INFOTAINMENT success verifies enrollment. Uncertain(error) / Failure(error).

Feature details and call order · Returned fields and error handling

Vehicle.addKeyRequest()

public fun addKeyRequest( publicKey: PublicKeyBytes, role: KeyRole = KeyRole.OWNER, formFactor: KeyFormFactor = platformKeyFormFactor(), timeout: Duration? = null): Flow<PairingEvent>

No authenticated session required. Connect over BLE first.

ParameterType / defaultMeaning, units and accepted values
publicKeyPublicKeyBytes
required		65-byte uncompressed P-256 public key: 0x04 || X(32) || Y(32).
roleKeyRole
KeyRole.OWNER		Role for the enrolled key; default OWNER. See models for every value.
formFactorKeyFormFactor
platformKeyFormFactor()		Key form factor. Platform default: Android=ANDROID_DEVICE, iOS=IOS_DEVICE, JVM=CLOUD_KEY.
timeoutDuration?
null		Whole-call time limit. null uses the config default: commands 5 s, handshake 20 s, connect 30 s, pairing 60 s.

Call example

vehicle.addKeyRequest(publicKey = key.publicKey, role = KeyRole.OWNER, formFactor = platformKeyFormFactor(), timeout = 10.seconds).collect { event ->
    // Handle each event in the UI.
}

Return value

Flow<PairingEvent>

Cold Flow: Sent/WaitingForTap/Unknown, then terminal Accepted/Rejected/Failed/TimedOut. Every collection sends a request; avoid duplicate collectors. Verify with sessionInfo(INFOTAINMENT) after Accepted.

Feature details and call order · Returned fields and error handling

Vehicle.addKey()

public suspend fun addKey( publicKey: PublicKeyBytes, role: KeyRole = KeyRole.OWNER, formFactor: KeyFormFactor = platformKeyFormFactor(), timeout: Duration? = null): VehicleResult<Unit>

Requires a VCSEC session: call after startSession(setOf(VehicleDomain.VCSEC)) succeeds. After an uncertain key change use listKeys(); for other controls use vehicleStatus().

ParameterType / defaultMeaning, units and accepted values
publicKeyPublicKeyBytes
required		65-byte uncompressed P-256 public key: 0x04 || X(32) || Y(32).
roleKeyRole
KeyRole.OWNER		Role for the enrolled key; default OWNER. See models for every value.
formFactorKeyFormFactor
platformKeyFormFactor()		Key form factor. Platform default: Android=ANDROID_DEVICE, iOS=IOS_DEVICE, JVM=CLOUD_KEY.
timeoutDuration?
null		Whole-call time limit. null uses the config default: commands 5 s, handshake 20 s, connect 30 s, pairing 60 s.

Call example

val result = vehicle.addKey(publicKey = key.publicKey, role = KeyRole.OWNER, formFactor = platformKeyFormFactor(), timeout = 10.seconds)

Return value

VehicleResult<Unit>

Success(Unit): vehicle acknowledgement with no data. Re-query when the UI needs the resulting state. Uncertain(error): it may have executed; check state instead of resending. Failure(error): act on its type.

Feature details and call order · Returned fields and error handling

Vehicle.setSeatCooler()

public suspend fun setSeatCooler( level: SeatClimateLevel, seat: SeatPosition, timeout: Duration? = null): VehicleResult<Unit>

Requires an INFOTAINMENT session and an awake vehicle. Call after startSession(setOf(VehicleDomain.INFOTAINMENT)) succeeds. Does not start a session or wake the vehicle automatically.

ParameterType / defaultMeaning, units and accepted values
levelSeatClimateLevel
required		Seat cooler: OFF/LOW/MED/HIGH. The sunroof Int level is an absolute position with no local range check.
seatSeatPosition
required		Cooler accepts only FRONT_LEFT/FRONT_RIGHT; otherwise InvalidArgument.
timeoutDuration?
null		Whole-call time limit. null uses the config default: commands 5 s, handshake 20 s, connect 30 s, pairing 60 s.

Call example

val result = vehicle.setSeatCooler(level = SeatClimateLevel.LOW, seat = SeatPosition.FRONT_RIGHT, timeout = 10.seconds)

public data class ProtocolFault(
    public val fault: MessageFault_E,
    ) : VehicleError
public data object KeyNotPaired : VehicleError
public data object InsufficientPrivileges : VehicleError
public data object Busy : VehicleError
public data class UnknownFault(
    public val rawCode: Int,
    ) : VehicleError
public data class UnknownKeychainCode(
    public val rawCode: Int,
    ) : VehicleError
public data object UnknownResponse : VehicleError
public data object NotConnected : VehicleError
public data object NoSession : VehicleError
public data object RequiresKey : VehicleError
public data class BadResponse(
    public val detail: String,
    override val mayHaveSucceeded: Boolean = false,
    ) : VehicleError
public data class KeychainRejected(
    public val code: WhitelistOperation_information_E,
    ) : VehicleError
public data class VcsecRejected(
    public val error: GenericError_E,
    ) : VehicleError
public data class UnknownVcsecError(
    public val rawCode: Int,
    ) : VehicleError
public data class InfotainmentRejected(
    public val reason: String,
    ) : VehicleError
public sealed interface TransportError : VehicleError
public data object ScanTimeout : TransportError
public data object MaxConnectionsExceeded : TransportError
public data class ConnectFailed(
    public val cause: String,
    ) : TransportError
public data object Disconnected : TransportError
public data object BluetoothOff : TransportError
public data object PermissionDenied : TransportError
public data class WriteFailed(
    public val cause: String,
    ) : TransportError
public sealed interface KeyStoreError : VehicleError
public data object HardwareUnavailable : KeyStoreError
public data object KeyNotFound : KeyStoreError
public data object Locked : KeyStoreError
public data class PlatformFailure(
    public val cause: String,
    ) : KeyStoreError
public data class Timeout(
    public val afterSend: Boolean,
    ) : VehicleError
public data class InvalidArgument(
    public val detail: String,
    ) : VehicleError

// Properties (including derived properties)
message: String
mayHaveSucceeded: Boolean
temporary: Boolean
fault: MessageFault_E
rawCode: Int
detail: String
code: WhitelistOperation_information_E
error: GenericError_E
reason: String
cause: String
afterSend: Boolean
RETRIABLE_FAULTS: Set<MessageFault_E>

VehicleEvent

public sealed interface VehicleEvent
public class Status(
    public val status: VehicleStatus,
    raw: ByteArray,
    unknownFields: List<String>,
    ) : VehicleEvent
public class AuthenticationRequest(
    public val requestedLevel: AuthenticationLevel,
    reasons: List<AuthenticationReason>,
    raw: ByteArray,
    unknownFields: List<String>,
    ) : VehicleEvent
public class Unrecognized(
    raw: ByteArray,
    unknownFields: List<String>,
    ) : VehicleEvent
public enum class AuthenticationLevel
    NONE, UNLOCK, DRIVE, UNKNOWN
public enum class AuthenticationReason
    NOT_DOCUMENTED, IDENTIFICATION, POWER_ON_VEHICLE_REQUEST, GTW_REQUEST, UI_UNLOCK_PASSIVE_AUTH, PASSIVE_UNLOCK_EXTERIOR_HANDLE_PULL, PASSIVE_UNLOCK_INTERIOR_HANDLE_PULL, PASSIVE_UNLOCK_AUTOPRESENT_DOOR, ENTERED_HIGHER_AUTH_ZONE, WALK_UP_UNLOCK, IMMOBILIZER, UNKNOWN

// Properties (including derived properties)
raw: ByteArray
unknownFields: List<String>
status: VehicleStatus
requestedLevel: AuthenticationLevel
reasons: List<AuthenticationReason>

VehicleResult

public sealed interface VehicleResult<out T>
public data class Success<T>(
    public val value: T,
    ) : VehicleResult<T>
public data class Uncertain(
    public val error: VehicleError,
    ) : VehicleResult<Nothing>
public data class Failure(
    public val error: VehicleError,
    ) : VehicleResult<Nothing>

// Properties (including derived properties)
value: T
error: VehicleError

ChargeSchedule

message ChargeSchedule {
uint64                      id                      = 1; // datetime in epoch time
    string                      name                    = 2;
    int32                       days_of_week            = 3;
    bool                        start_enabled           = 4;
    int32                       start_time              = 5; // 24h in minutes
    bool                        end_enabled             = 6;
    int32                       end_time                = 7; // 24h in minutes
    bool                        one_time                = 8;
    bool                        enabled                 = 9;
    float                       latitude                = 10;
    float                       longitude               = 11;
}

PreconditionSchedule

message PreconditionSchedule {
uint64                      id                      = 1; // datetime in epoch time
    string                      name                    = 2;
    int32                       days_of_week            = 3;
    int32                       precondition_time       = 4; // 24h in minutes
    bool                        one_time                = 5;
    bool                        enabled                 = 6;
    float                       latitude                = 7;
    float                       longitude               = 8;
}

Full getState field schemas

Complete field declarations for VehicleData and the 12 queried state messages: field names, wire types, numbers and oneof alternatives. Schema presence does not promise a vehicle will send a field. Fields marked TESLEMETRY-EXT are unsupported; do not depend on them. Units, null handling and Kotlin/Swift access examples are in Reading state.

VehicleData

message VehicleData {
    GuiSettings gui_settings = 2;  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
    ChargeState charge_state = 3;
    ClimateState climate_state = 4;
    DriveState drive_state = 5;
    VehicleState legacy_vehicle_state = 6; // VehicleState carries the legacy vehicle-state surface  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
    VehicleConfig vehicle_config = 7;  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
    LocationState location_state = 8;
    ClosuresState closures_state = 9;
    oneof optional_proto_json_version { int32 proto_json_version = 10; }  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
    // Each entry is an encoded CarServer.EncryptedData (car_server.proto), declared
    // as bytes because vehicle.proto cannot import car_server.proto.
    repeated bytes encrypted_data = 11;  // TESLEMETRY-EXT UNCONFIRMED: not yet confirmed on a live vehicle
    oneof optional_upload_reason { string upload_reason = 12; }  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
    ParkedAccessoryState parked_accessory_state = 14;  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
    ChargeScheduleState charge_schedule_state = 15;
    PreconditioningScheduleState preconditioning_schedule_state = 16;
    SohState soh_state = 17;  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
    CurrentVehicleState vehicle_state = 18; // present on live vehicleDataSubscription pushes  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
    TirePressureState tire_pressure_state = 19;
    MediaState media_state = 20;
    MediaDetailState media_detail_state = 21;
    VehicleDetailState vehicle_detail_state = 22;  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
    SoftwareUpdateState software_update_state = 23;
    ParentalControlsState parental_controls_state = 24;
    AlertState alert_state = 25;  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
    LightShowState light_show_state = 26;  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
    VehicleImageState vehicle_image_state = 27;  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
    SuspensionState suspension_state = 29;  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
    ChildPresenceDetectionState child_presence_detection_state = 30;  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
    DisplayState display_state = 36;  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
    // Each entry is an encoded CarServer.PiiKeyResponse (car_server.proto), declared
    // as bytes because vehicle.proto cannot import car_server.proto.
    repeated bytes pii_key_responses = 900;  // TESLEMETRY-EXT UNCONFIRMED: not yet confirmed on a live vehicle; name not recovered
    // An encrypted envelope whose message type this repo does not publish.
    bytes wrapped_key = 901;  // TESLEMETRY-EXT UNCONFIRMED: not yet confirmed on a live vehicle
    oneof optional_supports_optional_fields { bool supports_optional_fields = 999; }  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
}

ChargeState

message ChargeState {
    message CableType {
        oneof type {
            Void SNA = 1;
            Void IEC = 2;
            Void SAE = 3;
            Void GB_AC = 4;
            Void GB_DC = 5;
        }
    }

    message ChargerType {
        oneof type {
            Void SNA = 1;
            Void Supercharger = 2;
            Void Chademo = 3;
            Void Gb = 4;
            Void ACSingleWireCAN = 5;
            Void Combo = 6;
            Void MCSingleWireCAN = 7;
            Void Other = 8;
            Void Tesla = 9;
        }
    }

    message ChargingState {
        oneof type {
            Void Unknown = 1;
            Void Disconnected = 2;
            Void NoPower = 3;
            Void Starting = 4;
            Void Charging = 5;
            Void Complete = 6;
            Void Stopped = 7;
            Void Calibrating = 8;
        }
    }

    message ChargerBrand {
        oneof type {
            Void Tesla = 1;
            Void SNA = 2;
        }
    }

    enum ScheduledChargingMode {
        ScheduledChargingModeOff = 0;
        ScheduledChargingModeStartAt = 1;
        ScheduledChargingModeDepartBy = 2;
    }

    enum ChargePortColor_E {
        ChargePortColorOff = 0;
        ChargePortColorRed = 1;
        ChargePortColorGreen = 2;
        ChargePortColorBlue = 3;
        ChargePortColorWhite = 4;
        ChargePortColorFlashingGreen = 5;
        ChargePortColorFlashingAmber = 6;
        ChargePortColorAmber = 7;
        ChargePortColorRave = 8;
        ChargePortColorDebug = 9;
        ChargePortColorFlashingBlue = 10;
    }

    enum ChargeLimitReason {
        ChargeLimitReasonUnknown = 0;
        ChargeLimitReasonNone = 1;
        ChargeLimitReasonEvse = 2;
        ChargeLimitReasonBattTempLow = 3;
        ChargeLimitReasonHighSoc = 4;
        ChargeLimitReasonCabin = 5;
        ChargeLimitReasonEvseRelocationRecommended = 6;  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
    }

    enum OutletState {
        OutletStateOff = 0;
        OutletStateCabinAndBed = 1;
        OutletStateCabin = 2;
    }

    enum PowerFeedState {
        PowerFeedStateOff = 0;
        PowerFeedStateCabinAndBed = 1;
        PowerFeedStateCabin = 2;
    }

    enum PowershareStatus {
        PowershareStatusInactive = 0;                // disconnected or connected, but don't know if powershare capable
        PowershareStatusInit    = 1;                 // initializing
        PowershareStatusActive  = 2;                 // actively powersharing
        PowershareStatusStopped = 3;                 // powersharing has stopped - either standby or faulted or blocked; look at stopReason
        PowershareStatusHandshaking = 4;             // powersharing is handshaking
        PowershareStatusActiveReconnectingSoon = 5;  // powersharing is active, but reconnecting soon
    }

    enum PowershareType {
        PowershareTypeNone = 0;
        PowershareTypeLoad = 1;
        PowershareTypeHome = 2;
        PowershareTypeGrid = 3;  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
        PowershareTypePowerwall = 4;  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
    }

    enum PowershareStopReason {
        PowershareStopReasonNone = 0;
        PowershareStopReasonSOCTooLow = 1;
        PowershareStopReasonRetry = 2;
        PowershareStopReasonFault = 3;
        PowershareStopReasonUser = 4;
        PowershareStopReasonReconnecting = 5;
        PowershareStopReasonAuthentication = 6;
        PowershareStopReasonAdapterUpdating = 7;  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
        PowershareStopReasonAdapterInitializationFailed = 8;  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
    }

    ChargingState charging_state = 1;
    ChargerType   fast_charger_type = 2;
    ChargerBrand  fast_charger_brand = 3;
    oneof optional_charge_limit_soc { int32         charge_limit_soc = 104; }
    oneof optional_charge_limit_soc_std { int32         charge_limit_soc_std = 105; }
    oneof optional_charge_limit_soc_min { int32         charge_limit_soc_min = 106; }
    oneof optional_charge_limit_soc_max { int32         charge_limit_soc_max = 107; }
    reserved 8;
    oneof optional_max_range_charge_counter { int32         max_range_charge_counter = 109; }
    oneof optional_fast_charger_present { bool          fast_charger_present = 110; }

    // 2 decimals
    oneof optional_battery_range { float         battery_range = 111; }
    oneof optional_est_battery_range { float         est_battery_range = 112; }
    oneof optional_ideal_battery_range { float         ideal_battery_range = 113; }

    oneof optional_battery_level { int32         battery_level = 114; }
    oneof optional_usable_battery_level { int32         usable_battery_level = 115; }

    // 1 decimal
    oneof optional_charge_energy_added { float         charge_energy_added = 116; }
    oneof optional_charge_miles_added_rated { float         charge_miles_added_rated = 117; }
    oneof optional_charge_miles_added_ideal { float         charge_miles_added_ideal = 118; }

    oneof optional_charger_voltage { int32         charger_voltage = 119; }
    oneof optional_charger_pilot_current { int32         charger_pilot_current = 120; }
    oneof optional_charger_actual_current { int32         charger_actual_current = 121; }

    oneof optional_minutes_to_full_charge { int32 minutes_to_full_charge = 123; }
    oneof optional_minutes_to_charge_limit { int32 minutes_to_charge_limit = 142; }
    oneof optional_trip_charging { bool          trip_charging = 125; }

    oneof optional_charge_rate_mph { int32         charge_rate_mph = 126; }
    oneof optional_charge_port_door_open { bool          charge_port_door_open = 127; }
    CableType     conn_charge_cable = 28;

    // seconds / datetime
    oneof optional_scheduled_charging_start_time { uint64        scheduled_charging_start_time = 129; }

ClimateState

message ClimateState {
    message ClimateKeeperMode {
        oneof type {
            Void Unknown = 1;
            Void Off = 2;
            Void On = 3;
            Void Dog = 4;
            Void Party = 5;
        }
    }

    message DefrostMode {
        oneof type {
            Void Off = 1;
            Void Normal = 2;
            Void Max = 3;
        }
    }

    enum HvacAutoRequest {
        HvacAutoRequestOn = 0;
        HvacAutoRequestOverride = 1;
    }

    enum CabinOverheatProtection_E {
        CabinOverheatProtectionOff = 0;
        CabinOverheatProtectionOn = 1;
        CabinOverheatProtectionFanOnly = 2;
    }

    enum SeatHeaterLevel_E {
        SeatHeaterLevelOff = 0;
        SeatHeaterLevelLow = 1;
        SeatHeaterLevelMed = 2;
        SeatHeaterLevelHigh = 3;
    }

    enum SeatCoolingLevel_E {
        SeatCoolingLevelOff = 0;
        SeatCoolingLevelLow = 1;
        SeatCoolingLevelMed = 2;
        SeatCoolingLevelHigh = 3;
    }

    enum CopActivationTemp {
        CopActivationTempUnspecified = 0;
        CopActivationTempLow = 1;
        CopActivationTempMedium = 2;
        CopActivationTempHigh = 3;
    }

    enum COPNotRunningReason {
        COPNotRunningReasonNoReason = 0;
        COPNotRunningReasonUserInteraction = 1;
        COPNotRunningReasonEnergyConsumptionReached = 2;
        COPNotRunningReasonTimeout = 3;
        COPNotRunningReasonLowSolarLoad = 4;
        COPNotRunningReasonFault = 5;
        COPNotRunningReasonCabinBelowThreshold = 6;
    }

    enum DogModeState {  // TESLEMETRY-EXT
        DogModeStateUnavailableFault = 0;
        DogModeStateUnavailableTooHot = 1;
        DogModeStateAvailable = 2;
        DogModeStateRunningNominal = 3;
        DogModeStateRunningFault = 4;
        DogModeStateRunningTemperatureMonitorTrip = 5;
    }

    oneof optional_inside_temp_celsius { float inside_temp_celsius = 101; }
    oneof optional_outside_temp_celsius { float outside_temp_celsius = 102; }
    oneof optional_driver_temp_setting { float driver_temp_setting = 103; }
    oneof optional_passenger_temp_setting { float passenger_temp_setting = 104; }

11. Library Overview

tesla-ble-kmp is the library built in this repository: a Kotlin Multiplatform port of the BLE path of vehicle-command, so an Android or iOS app can talk to a Tesla directly, with no server. Chapters 11 to 19 describe how to use it. Chapters 1 to 9 describe the Go original.

On this page
  1. What works today
  2. Platforms and packages
  3. Installation
  4. Which page to read
  5. How these pages are kept up to date

What works today

The library is built milestone by milestone. These pages describe the code on main: M0 to M5 (the protocol core, scanning and connecting, keys and pairing, reading state with getState() and all 51 infotainment commands), the broadcast events and the key accessibility choice from issue #91, and stage 1 of M6: the iOS Swift package.

AreaMilestoneStatePage
Protocol core, dispatcher, sessions, retries, session cacheM0–M2Done16, 19
BLE scan and connect (Kable), key store, session cache storage, TeslaBle / VehicleM3Done real-vehicle check on iOS 2026-10-18; Android real device not checked12, 17
Read-only queries without a key: vehicleStatus(), keySummary(), listKeys()M3Done14
Key enrollment (addKeyRequest, sessionInfo, addKey, removeKey), VCSEC control (lock, unlock, trunk…)M4Done real-vehicle check pending13, 15
Broadcast events vehicle.events (VehicleEvent) and VehicleStatus.gear#91 (before M5)Done the broadcasts were seen on a real car; the events API itself is checked with FakeVehicle tests, real-vehicle check pending14, 12
Key accessibility for a locked iPhone: KeyAccessibility, VehicleKey.accessibility, KeyStoreError.Locked; startSession() also returns Locked, without retrying, when the phone lock blocks a key read earlier, and the saved session is kept (#96)#91 (before M5)Done checked with unit tests and fakes; the Keychain round trip, the Secure Enclave error on a locked iPhone and use on a locked iPhone are checked later on a device13, 16, 17
Infotainment state query getState(category), 12 categoriesM5 (ahead of the rest)Done checked with FakeVehicle tests; real-vehicle check pending (#118)14, 16
Connect scan observation Vehicle.scanObservation (time to the scan hit, signal strength, open attempt) and the [connect:scan-hit] observation log line (FR-038)#134 (not part of M5)Done checked with fake connector tests; real-vehicle check pending12, 18
Saved session counter reserved before use: after the app ended without disconnect(), the first request of the next launch is no longer refused once (FR-017, ADR-0016; no API change)H17 (not part of M5)Done checked with FakeVehicle tests; real-vehicle check pending17, 19, 16
Infotainment commands: 51 methods for climate, charging and power, the charge port, windows and sunroof, horn and lights, sentry and valet mode, media, software updates and ping() (FR-034, FR-050–053, FR-060–085, FR-090–092)M5Done checked with FakeVehicle tests and a Swift typecheck; real-vehicle check pending (#118)15, 16, 17
Climate and charging in the sample apps (SFR-001, SFR-002, SFR-025)M5Done Android and iOS sample unit tests; real-device check pending (#118)Sample app specification (samples/documents/)

Packages for Android and JVM: Maven CentralM6 stage 2Planned development is complete, but the check on a real Android device is not finished. Build from source until thenInstallation
Planned APIs are not available yet

Names of planned methods come from the requirements document (docs/prd/PRD.md) and may change when the milestone lands. Today an app can scan, connect, read state, read infotainment state with getState(), receive the car's broadcasts, enroll a key, choose whether the key works while the phone is locked, and send the VCSEC control commands and every infotainment command. Pairing, the VCSEC commands, getState() and the infotainment commands have not been checked on a real car yet.

Platforms and packages

  • Android 12 (API 31) or later and iOS 16 or later. The app is expected to be in the foreground.
  • A JVM target exists for tests and experiments. Its keys and session cache live in memory only.
  • Package root dev.mirolab.teslable: the facade in dev.mirolab.teslable, values and results in dev.mirolab.teslable.model, the key store, scanning and logging in dev.mirolab.teslable.port. Wire generated protocol types are in com.tesla.generated.*, for example com.tesla.generated.carserver.vehicle.VehicleData.
  • The iOS framework is Teslable. Swift sees Kotlin through SKIE (async functions, AsyncSequence, onEnum(of:)). It ships as a Swift package: a static XCFramework for devices (ios-arm64) and for the simulator on Apple Silicon Macs (ios-arm64-simulator).
  • Android and JVM are not published yet (Maven Central in stage 2 of M6). Build them from source.

Installation

iOS: Swift Package Manager

The iOS library is a Swift package. The Package.swift at the root of the repository points at a static Teslable.xcframework attached to each GitHub Release, so the package URL is the repository URL.

  1. In Xcode choose File > Add Package Dependencies… and enter https://github.com/smallmiro/tesla-ble-kmp.
  2. Choose the dependency rule Up to Next Major Version from 1.0.0, and add the product Teslable to your app target.
  3. Write import Teslable in your Swift code.

In a Package.swift, use these two lines:

.package(url: "https://github.com/smallmiro/tesla-ble-kmp", from: "1.0.0")
// in the target's dependencies:
.product(name: "Teslable", package: "tesla-ble-kmp")
  • Requirements: iOS 16 or later, and NSBluetoothAlwaysUsageDescription in Info.plist. The framework is static.
  • Simulator: Apple Silicon Macs only. A simulator build of every architecture, such as a Release build, needs EXCLUDED_ARCHS[sdk=iphonesimulator*] = x86_64. See 17. Platform Notes.
  • Versions follow semantic versioning: a release that breaks the public API is a new major version, so Up to Next Major Version takes only compatible releases.
  • CocoaPods is not supported. The CocoaPods trunk is read-only from 2026-12-02.

Step by step, with a complete package manifest: 12. Getting Started.

Build from source (Android, JVM, contributors)

Android and JVM are not published yet. Development is complete, but the check on a real Android device is not finished; Maven Central comes in stage 2 of M6. Until then, build inside this repository's Gradle build (12. Getting Started):

TargetCommand
Android / JVM./gradlew :sdk:assemble
iOS simulator framework, for work on the library and the sample app./gradlew :sdk:linkDebugFrameworkIosSimulatorArm64
Sample appssamples/android (Gradle module), samples/ios (xcodegen generate, see samples/ios/README.md)
Real-vehicle check pending

Version 1.0.0 includes features that pass the FakeVehicle tests but are not confirmed on a real car yet, notably the infotainment commands and getState() (checklist: issue #118). Android has not been checked on a real device yet. The table in What works today gives the state of each area.

How these pages are kept up to date

Every pull request that changes the library's behaviour, public API or milestone status updates these pages before it is merged: the Markdown page for agents (documents/md/1x-lib-*.md, English) and the HTML page for people in Korean, English and Japanese. The Go reference chapters 1 to 9 are changed only with the maintainer's approval.

12. Getting Started with the Library

Starts with adding the library to your app, then walks through the whole flow: create the library, get a key, find the car, connect, read its state and disconnect. The last two sections show pairing a new key and sending the first command (M4), and receiving the car's broadcasts.

On this page
  1. Add the library
  2. The flow
  3. Five rules
  4. Kotlin (Android)
  5. Swift (iOS)
  6. Connect scan observation
  7. Pair, then lock
  8. Broadcast events
  9. Next steps

Add the library

iOS: Swift Package Manager

  1. In Xcode choose File > Add Package Dependencies… and enter https://github.com/smallmiro/tesla-ble-kmp.
  2. Choose the dependency rule Up to Next Major Version from 1.0.0, and add the library product Teslable to your app target.
  3. Set the app's deployment target to iOS 16.0 or later, and add NSBluetoothAlwaysUsageDescription to Info.plist (Platform Notes).
  4. Use the simulator on an Apple Silicon Mac. If a simulator build builds every architecture, for example a Release build, set EXCLUDED_ARCHS[sdk=iphonesimulator*] = x86_64 on the app target.
  5. Write import Teslable and continue with Swift (iOS) below.

A Swift package that depends on the library:

// swift-tools-version:5.9
import PackageDescription

let package = Package(
    name: "VehicleFeature",
    platforms: [.iOS(.v16)],
    products: [.library(name: "VehicleFeature", targets: ["VehicleFeature"])],
    dependencies: [
        .package(url: "https://github.com/smallmiro/tesla-ble-kmp", from: "1.0.0"),
    ],
    targets: [
        .target(name: "VehicleFeature", dependencies: [.product(name: "Teslable", package: "tesla-ble-kmp")]),
    ]
)
  • The package has one product, Teslable: a static XCFramework for devices (ios-arm64) and for the simulator on Apple Silicon Macs (ios-arm64-simulator). There is nothing to build from source.
  • In .product(name:package:), package is the identity taken from the URL, tesla-ble-kmp, not the package name.
  • Up to Next Major Version accepts 1.x releases only; a release that breaks the public API is a new major version.
  • The sample app in this repository does not use the package: it links a framework built from source (samples/ios/README.md).
  • CocoaPods is not supported.

Android and JVM: build from source

Not published yet: development is complete, the check on a real Android device is not finished, and Maven Central comes in stage 2 of M6. Until then, the verified way is an app module inside this repository's Gradle build that depends on :sdk. Copying the :sdk folder alone does not build, because it needs the other library modules and the build plugins.

git clone https://github.com/smallmiro/tesla-ble-kmp
cd tesla-ble-kmp
./gradlew :sdk:assemble
./gradlew :samples:android:assembleDebug
// build.gradle.kts of an app module included in this repository's settings.gradle.kts
dependencies {
    implementation(project(":sdk"))
}

Set the app's minSdk to 31 or higher. Permissions and backup rules: Platform Notes.

The flow

  1. Create the library. TeslaBle.create(platform, config), once per app.
  2. Get a key. ble.keys.getOrCreate() returns the phone's P-256 key, creating it in the secure hardware the first time.
  3. Find the car. ble.scan(vin) emits the car's advertisements; take the first one.
  4. Connect. ble.connect(vin, key, advertisement) returns a Vehicle.
  5. Read. vehicleStatus(), keySummary(), listKeys().
  6. Clean up. vehicle.disconnect(), then ble.close() when the app is done.

Five rules

  • Results are values. Every call to the car or the key store returns a VehicleResult: Success, Uncertain or Failure. Nothing is thrown, except when your own coroutine is cancelled.
  • One TeslaBle per app. Call close() when you are done; it also disconnects any car still connected.
  • Always disconnect. Call vehicle.disconnect() even if the link already dropped. It saves the session cache, erases session keys and frees the car's BLE slot. Calling it twice is fine.
  • A key is optional for reading. connect(vin, key = null) is enough for the three queries; starting a session needs a key.
  • Debug logging only in development. DebugLogger() writes to the platform log; leave it out of release builds.

Kotlin (Android)

import dev.mirolab.teslable.*
import dev.mirolab.teslable.model.*
import dev.mirolab.teslable.port.*
import kotlinx.coroutines.flow.first

suspend fun demo(context: android.content.Context, vinText: String) {
    val vin = Vin.parseOrNull(vinText) ?: return              // 17-character format check
    val ble = TeslaBle.create(
        PlatformContext(context),
        TeslaBleConfig.default(logger = DebugLogger()),         // development logger, not for release builds
    )
    try {
        val key = when (val r = ble.keys.getOrCreate()) {       // default alias, hardware first
            is VehicleResult.Success -> r.value
            is VehicleResult.Uncertain -> return
            is VehicleResult.Failure -> return                  // r.error: KeyStoreError
        }

        // Take the first advertisement. The caller sets the time limit (for example withTimeoutOrNull).
        val event = ble.scan(vin).first()
        val target = (event as? ScanEvent.Found)?.advertisement ?: return   // on Failed, see event.error

        val vehicle = when (val r = ble.connect(vin, key, target)) {
            is VehicleResult.Success -> r.value
            is VehicleResult.Uncertain -> return
            is VehicleResult.Failure -> return                  // r.error: TransportError and others
        }
        try {
            println(vehicle.vehicleStatus())                    // show it in the UI instead of println
            println(vehicle.listKeys())
        } finally {
            vehicle.disconnect()                                // frees the slot; call it even after the link dropped
        }
    } finally {
        ble.close()                                             // suspend; disconnects any vehicle still connected
    }
}

Request BLUETOOTH_SCAN and BLUETOOTH_CONNECT at runtime before these calls. See Platform Notes.

Swift (iOS)

SKIE turns suspend functions into async throws, Flow into AsyncSequence, and lets switch onEnum(of:) cover every case of a result. The code has the same shape as samples/ios/Sources/SampleModel.swift.

import Teslable

let ble = TeslaBle.companion.create(
    platform: PlatformContext(),
    config: TeslaBleConfig.companion.default(logger: DebugLogger())
)

func run(vinText: String) async {
    guard let vin = Vin.companion.parseOrNull(value: vinText) else { return }   // bad format

    // 1) Key
    var key: VehicleKey?
    do {
        let result = try await ble.keys.getOrCreate(alias: VehicleKeyStoreCompanion.shared.DEFAULT_ALIAS, policy: .preferHardware)
        switch onEnum(of: result) {
        case .success(let s): key = s.value as? VehicleKey
        case .uncertain(let u): print("uncertain: \(u.error.message)")
        case .failure(let f): print("failure: \(f.error.message)")
        }
    } catch { print("error: \(error)") }

    // 2) Scan (stop at the first advertisement; set a time limit by cancelling the Task)
    for await event in ble.scan(vin: vin) {
        switch onEnum(of: event) {
        case .found(let f): print("\(f.advertisement.localName.value) rssi=\(f.advertisement.rssi)")
        case .failed(let e): print("scan failed: \(e.error.message)")
        }
        break
    }

    // 3) Connect and query
    do {
        let connected = try await ble.connect(vin: vin, key: key, target: nil, options: ConnectOptions(timeout: nil))
        switch onEnum(of: connected) {
        case .success(let s):
            guard let vehicle = s.value as? Vehicle else { return }
            let status = try await vehicle.vehicleStatus(timeout: nil)
            switch onEnum(of: status) {
            case .success(let v): print("status: \(String(describing: v.value))")
            case .uncertain(let u): print("uncertain: \(u.error.message)")   // query again, do not resend
            case .failure(let f): print("failure: \(f.error.message)")
            }
            try await vehicle.disconnect()
        case .uncertain(let u): print("uncertain: \(u.error.message)")
        case .failure(let f): print("failure: \(f.error.message)")
        }
    } catch { print("error: \(error)") }
}

With target: nil, connect scans by itself; Connect scan observation below tells when it found the car. Add NSBluetoothAlwaysUsageDescription to Info.plist; see Platform Notes.

To pass a time limit from Swift, use the boxed helper and the seconds-based config factory. Every argument is required, because Swift gets no default-argument overloads.

let status = try await vehicle.vehicleStatus(timeout: Timeouts.shared.seconds(seconds: 10))
let config = TeslaBleConfig.companion.withSeconds(logger: DebugLogger(), commandTimeoutSeconds: 10,
    handshakeTimeoutSeconds: 20, connectTimeoutSeconds: 30, pairingTimeoutSeconds: 90)

Connect scan observation

When connect scans by itself, vehicle.scanObservation (FR-038) tells what it saw of the advertisement the link opened through: when it arrived and how strong the signal was. Use it to read slow connects and reconnects, instead of scanning first in your app, which would change the connection you are measuring. It is a reading only and changes nothing in how the link is opened. The Go original has no such API.

FieldKotlin / SwiftMeaning
hitAfterMillisLong / Int64Milliseconds from the start of connect until the library received that advertisement, earlier attempts and the waits between them included
rssiInt? / KotlinInt?Signal strength of that advertisement in dBm. null when the platform gave no value (iOS reserves 127, the JVM stack gives Int.MIN_VALUE). VehicleAdvertisement.rssi keeps those raw numbers
openAttemptInt / Int32Which open attempt made this link, from 1. Above 1 means earlier opens failed, not that the car was scanned again
  • With target = null there is always a value (a test fake has none). Each retry scans again, and the value describes the last scan, the one the link opened from.
  • With a target from ble.scan(vin) no scan runs, so the value is null. Your app already holds that advertisement.
  • With a target the library no longer remembers (it keeps the last 16 it saw, per TeslaBle), connect scans by itself, so there is a value and retries reuse that hit. So null means "this call has no observation of the advertisement it used", not "no scan ran".
  • It belongs to the connection. The value never changes and can still be read after disconnect().
  • Only on success. A failed connect returns no Vehicle. Scan hits of failed attempts are in the observation log.
  • It is not a wall-clock time. It is the time the library received the advertisement on the monotonic clock, not a radio timestamp, and that clock can stop while the device sleeps (Platform Notes). The start time plus hitAfterMillis is therefore not reliable. For a wall-clock time, use the arrival time of the [connect:scan-hit] log line.
  • Safe to log. It has no advertised name and no device identifier, and toString() prints only the three fields. The advertised name comes from the VIN, and a VIN mask does not catch it.

Kotlin

val vehicle = (ble.connect(vin, key) as VehicleResult.Success).value           // target = null: connect scans
vehicle.scanObservation?.let { scan ->
    log("scan hit after ${scan.hitAfterMillis} ms, rssi ${scan.rssi}, open attempt ${scan.openAttempt}")
}

Swift

// vehicle from ble.connect(vin: vin, key: key, target: nil, options: ConnectOptions(timeout: nil))
if let scan = vehicle.scanObservation {
    let rssi = scan.rssi.map { String($0.int32Value) } ?? "unavailable"
    print("scan hit after \(scan.hitAfterMillis) ms, rssi \(rssi), open attempt \(scan.openAttempt)")
}

hitAfterMillis is a plain number because a non-null Duration does not reach Swift as a usable value.

Pair, then lock

The first time with a new phone key: enroll it with the key card, wait until the car lists it, start the VCSEC session and send a command. Event handling is explained in 13. Keys and Pairing, the commands in 15. Commands.

Kotlin

// vehicle was connected with `key` (see above)
vehicle.addKeyRequest(key.publicKey).collect { event -> render(event) }      // user taps the key card, confirms on screen
if (waitUntilEnrolled(vehicle, key)) {                                       // polls sessionInfo(…, INFOTAINMENT), see chapter 13
    vehicle.startSession(setOf(VehicleDomain.VCSEC))
    val result = vehicle.lock()                                              // VehicleResult<Unit>; Uncertain: vehicleStatus(), do not resend
}

Swift

for await event in vehicle.addKeyRequest(publicKey: key.publicKey, role: .owner, formFactor: .iosDevice, timeout: nil) {
    render(event)                                                            // user taps the key card, confirms on screen
}
// poll vehicle.sessionInfo(publicKey:domain:timeout:) until success (chapter 13), then:
_ = try await vehicle.startSession(domains: [.vcsec], timeout: nil)
let result = try await vehicle.lock(timeout: nil)

In Swift every argument is passed explicitly, because Kotlin default arguments do not become Swift overloads.

Broadcast events

The car pushes plaintext broadcasts by itself: its state (lock, doors and other openings, gear, presence, sleep) and authentication requests. vehicle.events delivers them as a Flow<VehicleEvent>; no key and no session are needed. Which frames count, the event types, unknownFields and the buffer are explained in 14. Reading Vehicle State, B2.

  • Collect it on its own. Use a separate coroutine (in Swift, a separate Task). The flow ends by itself when the link drops or disconnect() is called, and a collection started after that ends at once. Then look at connection and call disconnect(). A new connection gives a new Vehicle with new events.
  • Only what arrives while you listen. Earlier broadcasts are not replayed. A repeat is not a change: the car also resends an unchanged status, 60 s after the previous one while awake with a person present (Likely, 14, B3.1), so compare with the last value.
  • Trunk: a trunk opened or closed by hand from outside is broadcast, but with the interior button it is not; when the trunk state matters, read it with vehicleStatus().
  • raw is the whole frame. It is not VIN-masked, and each access makes a new copy: read it once and mask the VIN before you store or share it.

Kotlin

// vehicle was connected as above (key = null is enough)
scope.launch {
    vehicle.events.collect { event ->
        when (event) {
            is VehicleEvent.Status -> render(event.status)                        // a repeat is not a change
            is VehicleEvent.AuthenticationRequest -> note(event.requestedLevel, event.reasons)
            is VehicleEvent.Unrecognized -> keep(event.raw, event.unknownFields)  // mask the VIN in raw before storing it
        }
    }
    // completed: the link dropped or disconnect() was called. Check vehicle.connection, then call vehicle.disconnect().
}

Swift

SKIE flows do not throw, so for await is enough. Write AuthenticationLevel.none with the type name: where the value is optional (for example a saved last level), a bare .none means nil. In Swift the public VehicleStatus model is called VehicleStatus_, because the generated VCSEC message has the plain name, so s.status is a VehicleStatus_.

// in its own Task; the loop ends by itself when the link drops or disconnect() is called
for await event in vehicle.events {
    switch onEnum(of: event) {
    case .status(let s):
        render(s.status)                                         // a repeat is not a change
    case .authenticationRequest(let a):
        if a.requestedLevel != AuthenticationLevel.none {
            print("authentication request: \(a.requestedLevel), reasons \(a.reasons)")
        }
    case .unrecognized(let u):
        let raw = u.raw                                          // every access copies the bytes: read it once
        keep(raw, u.unknownFields)                               // mask the VIN in raw before storing it
    }
}
// the loop ended: check vehicle.connection, then call vehicle.disconnect()

Next steps

  • Key enrollment and the pairing events: 13. Keys and Pairing. connect(vin, key = null) is still enough for vehicleStatus(), keySummary() and listKeys().
  • Watch the link with vehicle.connection and the sessions with vehicle.sessions, both StateFlows. When the link reports Disconnected, still call disconnect() yourself.
  • When connect found the car and how strong the signal was: vehicle.scanObservation (Connect scan observation above).
  • Live state from the car's broadcasts: vehicle.events (Broadcast events above, 14. Reading Vehicle State, B2).

13. Keys and Pairing

The phone needs its own key pair, and the car must list the public key before it accepts commands from it. The library creates and stores the key, reads the car's key list, and enrolls the key on the car: the user taps an NFC key card and confirms on the screen.

On this page
  1. The key store
  2. Where the key lives
  3. Using the key while the phone is locked
  4. Is my key enrolled?
  5. Deleting or replacing a key
  6. Enrolling a key
  7. Pairing events
  8. The pairing flow

The key store

TeslaBle.keys is a VehicleKeyStore. Keys are found by an alias; most apps only need the default one.

CallReturnsWhat it does
getOrCreate(alias, policy)VehicleResult<VehicleKey>Returns the stored key, or creates a NIST P-256 key pair under the alias that works only while the phone is unlocked (WHEN_UNLOCKED)
getOrCreate(alias, policy, accessibility)VehicleResult<VehicleKey>The same, but a new key gets the chosen accessibility (see below). An existing key is returned unchanged
get(alias)VehicleResult<VehicleKey?>Success(null) when there is no key
delete(alias)VehicleResult<Unit>Also succeeds when there is no key
  • The default alias is "teslable-default" (VehicleKeyStore.DEFAULT_ALIAS; in Swift VehicleKeyStoreCompanion.shared.DEFAULT_ALIAS).
  • An alias may use letters, digits, ., _ and -, up to 64 characters, and may not be . or ...
  • A VehicleKey is a handle. It exposes alias, protection, accessibility and publicKey (65 bytes, 0x04 ‖ X ‖ Y). The private key never leaves the platform key store, and there is no export API.
  • A VehicleKey has nothing to close. Keep it while you use it, then simply let it go. On iOS and Android each get or getOrCreate returns a new handle to the same stored key (the in-memory store returns the same one), and handles that nothing refers to any more are cleaned up automatically, so reading the key again before each connection does not pile up handles. A connected Vehicle keeps its own reference to its key.
  • To start a session the library calls the handle's key agreement function, sharedX; apps do not call it. When the phone lock blocks the key it throws KeyLockedException, and the library turns that into KeyStoreError.Locked (see below).

Where the key lives

The policy argument decides whether software keys are acceptable. VehicleKey.protection tells you where the key actually ended up.

KeyPolicyBehaviour
PREFER_HARDWARE (default)Hardware first, software if hardware fails
REQUIRE_HARDWAREHardware only; otherwise KeyStoreError.HardwareUnavailable
SOFTWARE_ONLYSoftware only, for tests and emulators
KeyProtectionHardwareWhere
SECURE_ENCLAVEyesiOS Secure Enclave
STRONGBOXyesAndroid StrongBox
TEEyesAndroid Trusted Execution Environment
SOFTWAREnoWrapped by Android Keystore or kept in the iOS Keychain; in memory on the JVM

If a real device reports SOFTWARE, consider telling the user: the key is weaker than a hardware key.

Using the key while the phone is locked

When a key is created you choose whether it can be used while the phone is locked. The choice only matters on iOS. The default keeps the earlier behaviour; an app that watches the car in the background, such as the monitor sample, opts in.

KeyAccessibilityiOS Keychain classUsable while lockedFor
WHEN_UNLOCKED (default)kSecAttrAccessibleWhenUnlockedThisDeviceOnlynoMost apps. While the phone is locked this key cannot be read, and its Secure Enclave key cannot start a session (Locked)
AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCKkSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnlyyes once the phone was unlocked after the last restartApps that must keep working in the background while the phone is locked
  • Pass the choice as the third argument: getOrCreate(alias, policy, accessibility), in Swift getOrCreate(alias:policy:accessibility:). The two-argument call creates WHEN_UNLOCKED keys.
  • Both settings keep the key on this phone: it never moves to another device through a backup or a device transfer.
  • The setting is fixed when the key is created. Asking for another value on an existing alias returns the existing key unchanged. key.accessibility gives the real value; on iOS it is read back from the stored key. If the Keychain does not report it (not expected), the key says WHEN_UNLOCKED and a WARN line is logged.
  • On Android and the JVM the argument is ignored and every key reports AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK: Android Keystore keys are created without an unlock or user-authentication requirement (per the API; not yet confirmed on a device).
  • On a locked iPhone, a key that only works unlocked, or any key before the first unlock after a restart, makes getOrCreate, get and delete return KeyStoreError.Locked. Never delete or re-create the key because of Locked. Try again after the phone is unlocked. If a new key cannot be created because the phone is locked, the library does not fall back to a weaker software key.
  • A Secure Enclave key that only works unlocked, read while the phone was unlocked and used after it locked, cannot start a session. startSession() returns KeyStoreError.Locked as soon as the car answers, for both parts of the car, and the library does not try again. If the car itself reports an error in that answer (for example, the key is not enrolled), that error comes first. Call startSession() again after the phone is unlocked, with the same key.
  • For the same reason, a saved session is not restored when you connect while the phone is locked. The saved session is kept: the library writes nothing to the session cache until that part of the car has a session again, so the first connection after the unlock restores it. Exception: if another saved session turned out to be outdated in the same connection, the library rewrites the cache without it, and the blocked session is dropped too (that part of the car makes a new handshake next time).
  • A software key (SOFTWARE) keeps its private key in memory, so a key object read before the phone locked keeps working after the lock (per the API, not confirmed on a device). Only reading it from the key store gives Locked.
  • Not yet confirmed on a device: that iOS reports the locked Secure Enclave key with the error code the library expects (-25308). If it reports another one, startSession() still ends with a timeout. Keep using one key object: reading it again from the key store while the phone is locked returns Locked.
  • A session that is already running keeps working when the screen locks, whatever the setting: commands in a live session do not use the private key. Only starting a new session, or restoring a saved one, needs it.
The risk of AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK

Once the phone has been unlocked after a restart, anyone holding the locked phone, or code running with the app's permissions, can use this key over Bluetooth for whatever its role allows, for example unlocking the car. The Secure Enclave stops the key from being copied, not from being used, and asks for no confirmation per command. Tell the user, show key.accessibility on screen, and consider a key with a limited role for monitoring. A software key (protection is SOFTWARE) is more exposed; use REQUIRE_HARDWARE when the app needs a hardware key.

Switching a key to the other setting

The setting cannot change, so switching means a new key under a new alias that is enrolled again:

  1. Choose a new alias, for example teslable-monitor. Keep the old key until the new one works.
  2. Call keys.getOrCreate(newAlias, KeyPolicy.PREFER_HARDWARE, KeyAccessibility.AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK) and check key.accessibility and key.protection.
  3. Enroll the new public key (Enrolling a key): with the NFC key card, or with addKey from a connection whose enrolled key may add keys.
  4. Confirm with sessionInfo(key.publicKey, VehicleDomain.INFOTAINMENT) or listKeys().
  5. If you wish, remove the old public key from the car (removeKey) and from the phone (keys.delete(oldAlias)).

Kotlin

val key = when (val r = ble.keys.getOrCreate("teslable-monitor", KeyPolicy.PREFER_HARDWARE, KeyAccessibility.AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK)) {
    is VehicleResult.Success -> r.value
    is VehicleResult.Failure ->
        if (r.error == VehicleError.KeyStoreError.Locked) return waitForUnlock()   // never delete or re-create here
        else return show("Key store: ${r.error.message}")
    is VehicleResult.Uncertain -> return                                          // the key store does not return it
}
if (key.accessibility != KeyAccessibility.AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK) {
    show("This key works only while the phone is unlocked. Create a key under a new alias and enroll it again.")
}

Swift

let r = try await ble.keys.getOrCreate(alias: "teslable-monitor", policy: .preferHardware, accessibility: .afterFirstUnlock)
switch onEnum(of: r) {
case .success(let s):
    guard let key = s.value as? VehicleKey else { return }
    if key.accessibility != .afterFirstUnlock { print("unlocked-only key: create a new alias and enroll it again") }
case .failure(let f):
    if f.error is VehicleErrorKeyStoreErrorLocked { print("phone locked: try again after unlock") }   // never delete
    else { print("key store: \(f.error.message)") }
case .uncertain: break                                                                               // not returned by the key store
}

Is my key enrolled?

vehicle.listKeys() works without a key and without waking the car. Compare the car's list with your public key:

val mine = key.publicKey.toByteArray()
val enrolled = when (val r = vehicle.listKeys()) {
    is VehicleResult.Success -> r.value.firstOrNull { it.publicKey?.toByteArray()?.contentEquals(mine) == true }
    else -> null                                   // Uncertain or Failure: unknown, ask again later
}
// enrolled?.role is the KeyRole, enrolled?.slot the whitelist slot

A key that is not enrolled can still connect and read. Starting a session with it fails with KeyNotPaired.

Deleting or replacing a key

  • keys.delete(alias) removes the key from the phone only. The car keeps listing the public key until it is removed on the car.
  • Saved sessions belong to one key. After the key changes, the next connection throws away the old sessions and starts a new handshake.
  • A corrupted stored key (KeyStoreError.PlatformFailure, stored key is corrupted) is never replaced on its own. Tell the user, call delete then getOrCreate, and enroll the new key on the car again. See Platform Notes.
  • KeyStoreError.Locked is not damage: the key is fine but the phone is locked. Do not delete it; try again after the phone is unlocked. The same holds for Locked from startSession().

Enrolling a key

The Go original sends the add-key request and does not read the reply. Reading the reply as PairingEvent values is new behaviour of this library and has not been checked on a real car yet.

CallReturnsWhat it does
addKeyRequest(publicKey, role, formFactor, timeout)Flow<PairingEvent>Asks the car to add the key (FR-022, FR-023). A cold flow: each collection sends one request. It is not signed, so no session is needed. timeout = null uses pairingTimeout (60 seconds). Requests run one at a time
sessionInfo(publicKey, domain, timeout)VehicleResult<SessionInfoSummary>Checks enrollment per domain (FR-024); the connection needs no key. Failure(KeyNotPaired) means the car does not list the key. Asking with the connection's own key also creates that domain's session, so sessions shows Ready(fromCache = false)
addKey(publicKey, role, formFactor, timeout)VehicleResult<Unit>An enrolled key with enough privileges adds another key without the NFC card (FR-027). Authenticated VCSEC; needs a VCSEC session
removeKey(publicKey, timeout)VehicleResult<Unit>Removes a key from the car (FR-026). Authenticated VCSEC; needs a VCSEC session
  • role defaults to KeyRole.OWNER; formFactor defaults to platformKeyFormFactor(): ANDROID_DEVICE on Android, IOS_DEVICE on iOS, CLOUD_KEY on the JVM (tests).
  • In Swift, Kotlin default arguments are not available: pass every argument (role:, formFactor:, timeout:), for example formFactor: .iosDevice.
  • addKey and removeKey can fail with InsufficientPrivileges (the connection's key may not manage keys, for example a Driver key), KeychainRejected(code) (for example WHITELIST_FULL), NoSession or InvalidArgument (role = UNKNOWN). Uncertain means the car may have changed its key list: check with listKeys() and do not resend. When a write failed and the library wrote the same bytes again, a refusal such as InsufficientPrivileges or KeyNotPaired (removing your own key makes the repeated copy's key unknown) comes back as Uncertain(Timeout), not Failure.

Pairing events

PairingEventMeaningLast event
SentThe request was written. The user must tap an NFC key card on the center console and confirm on the screenno
WaitingForTapThe car reports that it is waiting (WAIT)no
AcceptedThe car accepted the key. Infotainment can take tens of seconds more to learn ityes
Rejected(error)The car refused: KeychainRejected, UnknownKeychainCode, VcsecRejected or UnknownVcsecErroryes
Unknown(raw)A frame that could not be decoded. raw is a copy of the bytesno
Failed(error)Not sent (NotConnected, write failure, InvalidArgument), or the link dropped while waitingyes
TimedOutNo final answer within timeout. The key may still have been enrolled: check with sessionInfoyes

After a last event the flow ends (isTerminal is true for them). Frames that carry neither a status nor an error, intermediate OK statuses and frames received before the request was sent are ignored.

Not verified on a real car

The reply format of add-key-request has not been observed on a real car yet (ADR-0009). If you receive Unknown(raw), please report raw as hex (no VIN) together with what the car did.

The pairing flow

  1. Create the key (keys.getOrCreate()) and connect with it.
  2. Collect vehicle.addKeyRequest(key.publicKey). Tell the user to tap the key card on the center console and confirm on the screen.
  3. After Accepted (or TimedOut), call sessionInfo(key.publicKey, VehicleDomain.INFOTAINMENT) every 3 seconds for up to 2 minutes. KeyNotPaired means "not yet": keep polling. Success means the key is enrolled and synced.
  4. Run startSession(setOf(VehicleDomain.VCSEC)), then send commands (15. Commands).

Kotlin

val key = (ble.keys.getOrCreate() as VehicleResult.Success).value
val vehicle = (ble.connect(vin, key) as VehicleResult.Success).value

vehicle.addKeyRequest(key.publicKey).collect { event ->
    when (event) {
        PairingEvent.Sent -> show("Tap the key card on the center console, then confirm on the screen")
        PairingEvent.WaitingForTap -> show("Waiting for the card")
        PairingEvent.Accepted -> show("Accepted, waiting for the car to sync")
        is PairingEvent.Rejected -> show("Rejected: ${event.error.message}")
        is PairingEvent.Unknown -> report(event.raw.joinToString("") { it.toUByte().toString(16).padStart(2, '0') })      // see the warning below
        is PairingEvent.Failed -> show("Failed: ${event.error.message}")
        PairingEvent.TimedOut -> show("No answer, checking enrollment")
    }
}

suspend fun waitUntilEnrolled(vehicle: Vehicle, key: VehicleKey): Boolean {
    val deadline = TimeSource.Monotonic.markNow() + 2.minutes
    while (deadline.hasNotPassedNow()) {
        when (val r = vehicle.sessionInfo(key.publicKey, VehicleDomain.INFOTAINMENT)) {
            is VehicleResult.Success -> return true
            is VehicleResult.Failure -> if (r.error != VehicleError.KeyNotPaired) return false
            is VehicleResult.Uncertain -> Unit                  // reply lost: ask again
        }
        delay(3.seconds)
    }
    return false
}

Swift

for await event in vehicle.addKeyRequest(publicKey: key.publicKey, role: .owner, formFactor: .iosDevice, timeout: nil) {
    switch onEnum(of: event) {
    case .sent: print("Tap the key card on the center console, then confirm on the screen")
    case .waitingForTap: print("Waiting for the card")
    case .accepted: print("Accepted, waiting for the car to sync")
    case .rejected(let r): print("Rejected: \(r.error.message)")
    case .unknown(let u): print("Unknown frame, \(u.raw.size) bytes")
    case .failed(let f): print("Failed: \(f.error.message)")
    case .timedOut: print("No answer, checking enrollment")
    }
}

// then, every 3 s for up to 2 minutes:
let r = try await vehicle.sessionInfo(publicKey: key.publicKey, domain: .infotainment, timeout: nil)
switch onEnum(of: r) {
case .success: print("enrolled")
case .failure(let f): print("not yet or failed: \(f.error.message)")   // KeyNotPaired: keep polling
case .uncertain: print("reply lost, ask again")
}

Roles (KeyRole) are OWNER, DRIVER, FLEET_MANAGER, VEHICLE_MONITOR, CHARGING_MANAGER, GUEST and SERVICE. Which commands each role may send is listed in 7. Command Reference. Roles and key form factors that exist only in the extended protocol definitions (for example ROLE_RIDER or KEY_FORM_FACTOR_APPLE_WATCH) read as UNKNOWN.

14. Reading Vehicle State

Everything the library can read from the car over BLE, in two parts: Part A is data you ask for (four security-controller queries and the infotainment query getState()), Part B is data the car sends by itself (broadcasts). The four security-controller queries need no key, no session and no wake-up, because VCSEC answers even while the infotainment computer sleeps. getState() does need an enrolled key, a session and an awake car.

What exists today

The queries in Part A are implemented, including getState() (A8). It is checked with FakeVehicle tests, not yet on a real car. The car's broadcasts (Part B) reach your app through vehicle.events (B2); the observation log still shows them too.

On this page
  1. The queries
  2. A1. How a request travels
  3. A2. vehicleStatus()
  4. A3. keySummary()
  5. A4. listKeys()
  6. A5. sessionInfo()
  7. A6. Enum value tables
  8. A7. Other request types in the schema
  9. A8. getState()
  10. Result types
  11. Connection and session state
  12. Sleep and wake-up
  13. B1. What a broadcast is on the wire
  14. B2. Broadcast events: vehicle.events
  15. B3. Message catalogue
  16. B4. What is not available by broadcast
  17. B5. Observation method and environment
  18. B6. Provenance

The queries

CallReturnsGo originalFR
vehicleStatus(timeout = null)VehicleResult<VehicleStatus>BodyControllerState (InformationRequest{GET_STATUS})FR-031
keySummary(timeout = null)VehicleResult<WhitelistSummary>KeySummary (GET_WHITELIST_INFO)FR-032
listKeys(timeout = null)VehicleResult<List<WhitelistEntry>>KeySummary + KeyInfoBySlot per occupied slotFR-025
sessionInfo(publicKey, domain, timeout = null)VehicleResult<SessionInfoSummary>SessionInfo (session_info_request)FR-024
getState(category, timeout = null)VehicleResult<VehicleData>GetState (getVehicleData)FR-033
  • Each takes an optional timeout; without it the default command timeout (5 seconds) applies. For listKeys() the timeout covers all of its requests together.
  • The car handles one VCSEC request at a time per connection. A query that has to wait for another one spends part of its timeout waiting. getState() goes to infotainment and does not wait in that queue.
  • These are queries. An Uncertain result means no data was confirmed, not that a command may have run, so it is safe to ask again, just not in a tight loop (see 16. Error Handling).
  • While another Bluetooth client is active, a query can get no reply at all, listKeys() most often (16. Error Handling).
  • Control payloads (lock, trunk, wake-up, climate, charging, media and the other infotainment commands) are in 15. Commands.

A1. How a request travels

ItemValue
GATT service00000211-b2d1-43f0-9b88-960cebf8b91e
Write characteristic (phone to car)00000212-b2d1-43f0-9b88-960cebf8b91e
Notify/indicate characteristic (car to phone)00000213-b2d1-43f0-9b88-960cebf8b91e
Framing2-byte big-endian length prefix, then the serialized RoutableMessage. Maximum message 1024 bytes; a larger one fails with InvalidArgument
Write block lengthmin(ATT MTU, 1024) - 3; 20 if the MTU exchange fails

Every frame, in both directions, is a RoutableMessage (universal_message.proto):

FieldNumberTypeMeaning
to_destination6DestinationEither domain (1, enum Domain) or routing_address (2, bytes)
from_destination7DestinationSame shape. Requests carry our routing address; the car's replies carry the car's domain
protobuf_message_as_bytes10bytesPayload (one of): serialized domain message (UnsignedMessage or ToVCSECMessage to the car, FromVCSECMessage back)
session_info_request14SessionInfoRequestPayload (one of): public_key (1), challenge (2)
session_info15bytesPayload (one of): serialized Signatures.SessionInfo
signature_data13Signatures.SignatureDataPresent on authenticated (AES-GCM) commands and replies
signedMessageStatus12MessageStatusoperation_status (1), signed_message_fault (2, MessageFault_E)
request_uuid50bytesEcho of the request uuid (16 bytes). Used to match infotainment replies
uuid51bytesRequest id, 16 random bytes
flags52uint32Bit mask of Flags (FLAG_USER_COMMAND 0, FLAG_ENCRYPT_RESPONSE 1; 2 to 4 exist only in the Teslemetry schema)

Domain values: DOMAIN_BROADCAST 0, DOMAIN_VEHICLE_SECURITY 2 (VCSEC), DOMAIN_INFOTAINMENT 3. The Teslemetry schema also lists 4, 5, 7 and 8; the library does not use them. Its field 53 message_frame is unconfirmed and unused.

Routing rules for VCSEC requests:

  • to_destination.domain is DOMAIN_VEHICLE_SECURITY and from_destination.routing_address is a fresh random 16-byte address for every request. The reply comes back to that address and the library matches it by address (and the car's domain). request_uuid is not used to match VCSEC replies. The uuid (51) is still filled with 16 random bytes.
  • A reply addressed to any other routing address is not ours and is dropped as no-handler (see B3).
  • Queries are unauthenticated: no signature_data, and protobuf_message_as_bytes is the plain UnsignedMessage. Commands use the same envelope with signature_data and an AES-GCM protected payload.
  • For sessionInfo the payload is session_info_request (14) and to_destination.domain is the requested domain; the reply carries session_info (15).

A2. vehicleStatus()

suspend fun vehicleStatus(timeout: Duration? = null): VehicleResult<VehicleStatus>
ItemValue
AuthenticationNone (works with an unenrolled key, no session)
DomainDOMAIN_VEHICLE_SECURITY
Request payloadUnsignedMessage{InformationRequest{GET_STATUS}} = hex 0a00 (field 1, length 0; type 0 is the proto3 default and is not encoded)
Ends atThe first message of the reply
ReplyFromVCSECMessage.vehicleStatus (field 1)
Missing fieldFailure(BadResponse("missing vehicleStatus"))
ErrorsnominalError (46) becomes Failure(VcsecRejected(...)); a lost reply is Uncertain(Timeout(afterSend = true))

VCSEC.VehicleStatus (vcsec.proto):

#FieldTypeMeaningIn public VehicleStatus
1closureStatusesClosureStatusesState of each opening; only sent while something is openclosures; absent becomes all CLOSED
2vehicleLockStateVehicleLockState_ELock statelockState
3vehicleSleepStatusVehicleSleepStatus_EInfotainment sleep statesleepStatus
4userPresenceUserPresence_EA person in or at the car (also PRESENT while standing outside with a door open, B3.1)userPresence
5detailedClosureStatusDetailedClosureStatustonneauPercentOpen (1, uint32), CybertrucktonneauPercentOpen (null if absent)
6uiDesireUIDesire_E (Teslemetry)In the schema onlyNot surfaced (not planned while its meaning is not established; the observation log prints it)
7gearGear_E (Teslemetry; confirmed in broadcasts; Likely in query replies)Current geargear; absent or outside the schema becomes UNKNOWN
8(unknown, not in the schema)messageSeen on a real car: {1: 1|2, 2: 1|2, 3: 1|2}; meaning unknown (B3.1)Not surfaced

ClosureStatuses has eight ClosureState_E fields, numbered 1 to 8: frontDriverDoor, frontPassengerDoor, rearDriverDoor, rearPassengerDoor, rearTrunk, frontTrunk, chargePort, tonneau.

Mapping rules

A value outside the schema maps to UNKNOWN instead of the zero value. proto3 cannot tell a missing enum from its zero value, so a missing lock state reads as UNLOCKED; the library keeps that Go behaviour for the query. gear has no Go counterpart (the official Go schema ends at field 5); its zero value is GEAR_UNKNOWN, so a missing gear reads as UNKNOWN. Gear was confirmed in broadcasts. Query replies also fill it (Likely, B5): 144 of 144 carried gear; NEUTRAL not seen. The real-car results show the car omits closureStatuses when every field is CLOSED (the zero value) and sends it while any field is not (front and rear driver doors, rear trunk and charge port seen), so "absent means all closed" matches what we saw. The one exception is a trunk operated with the interior button, which sent no broadcast at all; this query should still report it (not yet checked on a real car).

7keyRoleKeys.RoleRolerole

A single-slot query exists on the internal query class but is not part of Vehicle.

A5. sessionInfo()

suspend fun sessionInfo(publicKey: PublicKeyBytes, domain: VehicleDomain, timeout: Duration? = null): VehicleResult<SessionInfoSummary>

A6. Enum value tables

Values come from vcsec.proto and universal_message.proto. A value the library does not know maps to UNKNOWN.
