Gear_E was confirmed on a real car: each of P, R, N and D produced one broadcast with the matching value. GEAR_UNKNOWN is the zero value and is never encoded, but frames with no gear field do occur and read as UNKNOWN (B2). UIDesire_E has no established meaning on a real car.

KeyFormFactor: the public model has only UNKNOWN, NFC_CARD, IOS_DEVICE, ANDROID_DEVICE and CLOUD_KEY. Wire values 0, 1, 6, 7 and 9 map to them. The Teslemetry snapshot values map to UNKNOWN: 2 3_BUTTON_BLE_CAR_KEYFOB, 3 BLE_DEVICE, 4 NFC_DEVICE, 5 BLE_AND_NFC_DEVICE, 8 3_BUTTON_BLE_CAR_KEYFOB_P60, 10 3_BUTTON_GEN2_CAR_KEYFOB_P60, 11 5_BUTTON_GEN2_CAR_KEYFOB_P60, 12 3_BUTTON_GEN2_CAR_KEYFOB_P60_V2, 13 3_BUTTON_GEN2_CAR_KEYFOB_P60_V3, 14 NFC_CARD_P71, 15 NFC_CARD_METAL_CARD, 16 NFC_CARD_PLASTIC_CARD, 17 APPLE_WATCH, 18 NFC_CARD_WD_PLASTIC_CARD, 19 HARMONY_OS_NEXT_DEVICE (each with the prefix KEY_FORM_FACTOR_).

A7. Other request types in the schema

InformationRequestType also defines GET_TOKEN 1, GET_COUNTER 2, GET_EPHEMERAL_PUBLIC_KEY 3, GET_SESSION_DATA 4, GET_PERSONALIZATION_INFO 7, GET_KEYSTATUS_INFO 8, GET_ACTIVE_KEY 9 and GET_CAPABILITIES 16 (Teslemetry only). The library sends none of them and has no API for them. getState(category) is not one of these: it goes to infotainment (A8).

A8. getState(category)

getState reads one of twelve infotainment categories, such as the battery, the climate, the speed or the location. It is checked with FakeVehicle tests; nothing in this section is confirmed on a real car yet (A8.5).

suspend fun getState(category: StateCategory, timeout: Duration? = null): VehicleResult<VehicleData>
ItemValue
AuthenticationAES-GCM over the infotainment session, with flags = 2 (FLAG_ENCRYPT_RESPONSE). Needs an enrolled key
DomainDOMAIN_INFOTAINMENT; the reply is matched by request_uuid
Request payloadCarServer.Action{vehicleAction{getVehicleData{selector}}} with exactly one selector (A8.1). CHARGE is hex 12040a021200
RequestsOne category per request. There is no way to ask for several at once
Ends atThe first reply
ReplyCarServer.Response.vehicleData, returned unchanged as the Wire generated type com.tesla.generated.carserver.vehicle.VehicleData
TimeoutWithout timeout, the default command timeout (5 seconds)
Retries16. Error Handling: temporary errors from the car are retried inside the timeout with a fresh counter, nonce and expiry, and a temporary write error writes the same bytes again; Uncertain is never resent. Unlike a command, a read keeps this retry after a failed write too: running it twice changes nothing
Results16. Error Handling

A8.1 Categories

StateCategory (dev.mirolab.teslable.model) has the 12 Go constants in the Go order. All 12 reply messages have a timestamp field. The full field lists are in the official pkg/protocol/protobuf/vehicle.proto of vehicle-command.

Only fields of the official proto are supported

VehicleData is generated from the Teslemetry snapshot, so it also has TESLEMETRY-EXT fields, for example gui_settings and vehicle_config, and native_latitude_d on LocationState. The library never requests them, and a value that arrives in one has no guaranteed meaning. Do not build on them. In this repository's domain/src/commonMain/proto/vehicle.proto they carry a TESLEMETRY-EXT comment (ADR-0002, amendment 2026-11-01).

A8.2 Call order

getState never starts a session and never wakes the car. Your app does both, in this order.

SituationWhat getState doesWhat your app does
No infotainment sessionSends nothing and returns Failure(NoSession)startSession(setOf(VehicleDomain.INFOTAINMENT)) first
A session restored from the cache that the car no longer acceptsReturns Failure(NoSession); sessions then shows None for INFOTAINMENTstartSession(setOf(VehicleDomain.INFOTAINMENT)), then ask again
Infotainment asleepSends one request, gets no reply and returns Uncertain(Timeout(afterSend = true))Wake it first, as below
 
Awake:    connect → startSession(setOf(INFOTAINMENT)) → getState(category)
Asleep:   connect → vehicleStatus() shows ASLEEP → startSession(setOf(VCSEC)) → wakeUp()
            → vehicleStatus() shows AWAKE → startSession(setOf(INFOTAINMENT)) → getState(category)
  • Decide with vehicleStatus(): it needs no session and never wakes the car.
  • On a sleeping car, handshake with VCSEC alone. The Go tool tesla-control does this for a BLE wake because infotainment is presumably asleep, and the Go recipes warn that a handshake with both domains can then take a long time.
  • wakeUp() returning Success means VCSEC accepted the command, not that infotainment is up. Wait for AWAKE, then start the infotainment session. The car can still answer busy right after waking; the library retries that inside the timeout, so the first query after a wake may need a longer timeout.
  • A saved session says nothing about sleep. startSession succeeds without asking the car and sessions shows Ready(fromCache = true) even while infotainment sleeps; the first query can then time out. Read sleepStatus instead.
  • Waking is your app's decision. The library never does it for you.
  • ping() (15. Commands) is a command that changes nothing and returns no data. Success shows that the key and the infotainment session work. It needs the same session and awake car as getState.
import dev.mirolab.teslable.Vehicle
import dev.mirolab.teslable.model.*
import kotlinx.coroutines.delay
import kotlin.time.Duration.Companion.seconds

// vehicle was connected with an enrolled key (12-lib-getting-started.md). True when getState can be called.
suspend fun prepareInfotainment(vehicle: Vehicle): Boolean {
    val status = vehicle.vehicleStatus()                                  // no session needed, never wakes the car
    if (status is VehicleResult.Success && status.value.sleepStatus == SleepStatus.ASLEEP) {
        if (vehicle.startSession(setOf(VehicleDomain.VCSEC)) !is VehicleResult.Success) return false
        if (vehicle.wakeUp() is VehicleResult.Failure) return false       // Uncertain: do not resend, the loop reads the state
        for (attempt in 1..30) {                                          // about 30 s; the real wake time is not confirmed (#118)
            delay(1.seconds)
            val now = vehicle.vehicleStatus()
            if (now is VehicleResult.Success && now.value.sleepStatus == SleepStatus.AWAKE) break
        }
    }
    return vehicle.startSession(setOf(VehicleDomain.INFOTAINMENT)) is VehicleResult.Success
}

func prepareInfotainment(vehicle: Vehicle) async throws -> Bool {
    let status = try await vehicle.vehicleStatus(timeout: nil)                 // no session needed, never wakes the car
    if case .success(let s) = onEnum(of: status), (s.value as? VehicleStatus_)?.sleepStatus == .asleep {
        let vcsec = try await vehicle.startSession(domains: [.vcsec], timeout: nil)
        guard case .success = onEnum(of: vcsec) else { return false }
        let woke = try await vehicle.wakeUp(timeout: nil)
        if case .failure = onEnum(of: woke) { return false }                   // uncertain: do not resend, the loop reads the state
        for _ in 1...30 {                                                      // about 30 s; the real wake time is not confirmed (#118)
            try await Task.sleep(nanoseconds: 1_000_000_000)
            let now = try await vehicle.vehicleStatus(timeout: nil)
            if case .success(let n) = onEnum(of: now), (n.value as? VehicleStatus_)?.sleepStatus == .awake { break }
        }
    }
    let session = try await vehicle.startSession(domains: [.infotainment], timeout: nil)
    if case .success = onEnum(of: session) { return true }
    return false
}

The 30 seconds comes from the Go BLE example and is not a measurement. Instead of polling, your app can watch sleepStatus in vehicle.events (B2).

A8.3 Reading the reply

  • Absent is not 0. Every scalar and message field directly on the 12 reply messages is nullable: a field the car did not send is null, and speed = 0 stays 0. Never replace null with 0. Repeated fields (charge_schedules, precondition_schedules) are empty lists when absent. Inside nested messages such as ChargeSchedule, fields are plain proto3 values: absent reads as 0, empty or false.
  • Check every field you use. A reply with no vehicleData is Success(VehicleData()), the same as an empty vehicleData, and the two cannot be told apart. Go returns (nil, nil) here; the library keeps a non-null result because your app checks each field anyway. Another kind of reply, such as Ping, gives the same empty result.
  • The reply is not checked against category (same as Go). Read the field for the category you asked for; a different field that arrives is passed through.
  • Success is not proof of a new measurement. Compare timestamp with the previous reply: if it did not move, do not present the values as fresh. If it is null, you cannot tell. timestamp is a Wire Instant: getEpochSecond() works in common Kotlin and in Swift.
  • Speed, power and odometer exist only in DRIVE, and that reply can go over the car's size limit (16. Error Handling). Live speed is not guaranteed, and there is no other source for it.
  • Wire keeps proto names, so fields are drive_state, speed_float, charge_state, unlike the camelCase hand-written models. Generated types are in com.tesla.generated.*.
  • Some states are wrapper messages, not enums: charging_state, climate_keeper_mode, sentry_mode_state and others carry one empty member that names the value (A8.6).
  • Do not log a result, toString() included. A VehicleData can hold the location and the destination. The library does not log it, and neither should your app.
import com.tesla.generated.universalmessage.MessageFault_E
import dev.mirolab.teslable.Vehicle
import dev.mirolab.teslable.model.*
import kotlin.time.Duration.Companion.seconds

suspend fun readSpeed(vehicle: Vehicle): Int? {
    when (val result = vehicle.getState(StateCategory.DRIVE)) {
        is VehicleResult.Success ->
            return result.value.drive_state?.speed               // null = the vehicle did not send it; 0 = it sent 0
        is VehicleResult.Uncertain -> {
            val e = result.error
            if (e is VehicleError.ProtocolFault && e.fault == MessageFault_E.MESSAGEFAULT_ERROR_RESPONSE_MTU_EXCEEDED) {
                return null                                      // reply too large
            }
            if (e is VehicleError.Timeout) return null           // no reply
            return null
        }
        is VehicleResult.Failure -> {
            if (result.error == VehicleError.NoSession) return null
            return null
        }
    }
}

// With an explicit timeout, and reading the reply's timestamp:
suspend fun readBattery(vehicle: Vehicle, lastSeconds: Long?) {
    val result = vehicle.getState(StateCategory.CHARGE, timeout = 8.seconds)
    if (result !is VehicleResult.Success) return
    val charge = result.value.charge_state ?: return             // no charge data in this reply
    val level: Int? = charge.battery_level
    val seconds: Long? = charge.timestamp?.getEpochSecond()
    val fresh = seconds != null && seconds != lastSeconds
    render(level, fresh)
}

Swift passes every argument. s.value needs a cast and numbers bridge as KotlinInt?; see 17. Platform Notes. This form passes a Swift typecheck against the framework; it has not run in a Swift program.

func readSpeed(vehicle: Vehicle) async throws -> Int32? {
    let result = try await vehicle.getState(category: .drive, timeout: nil)
    switch onEnum(of: result) {
    case .success(let s):
        // VehicleResult's generic is erased in Swift: s.value is AnyObject?, so cast it.
        guard let data = s.value as? VehicleData else { return nil }
        // Nullable numerics bridge as KotlinInt? / KotlinFloat?, not Int32? / Float?.
        let speed: KotlinInt? = data.drive_state?.speed
        let speedFloat: KotlinFloat? = data.drive_state?.speed_float
        _ = speedFloat?.floatValue
        return speed?.int32Value // nil = the vehicle did not send it; 0 = it sent 0
    case .uncertain(let u):
        switch onEnum(of: u.error) {
        case .protocolFault(let p) where p.fault == .messagefaultErrorResponseMtuExceeded:
            return nil // reply too large
        case .timeout(let t):
            _ = t.afterSend
            return nil // no reply
        default:
            return nil
        }
    case .failure(let f):
        if case .noSession = onEnum(of: f.error) { return nil }
        return nil
    }
}

// With an explicit timeout, and reading the reply's timestamp:
func readBattery(vehicle: Vehicle, lastSeconds: Int64?) async throws {
    let result = try await vehicle.getState(category: .charge, timeout: Timeouts.shared.seconds(seconds: 8))
    guard case .success(let s) = onEnum(of: result), let data = s.value as? VehicleData else { return }
    guard let charge = data.charge_state else { return }   // no charge data in this reply
    let level: KotlinInt? = charge.battery_level
    let seconds: Int64? = charge.timestamp?.getEpochSecond()
    let fresh = seconds != nil && seconds != lastSeconds
    render(level?.int32Value, fresh)
}

A8.4 Location: which coordinates to use

LOCATION can return several coordinate pairs. The rule is the LocationState comment in the official vehicle.proto, which Go GetState points to.

UseFieldsWhen
First choicenative_latitude, native_longitudeNormally. They use WGS (international) or GCJ (China) depending on the car's region; native_type says which
Fallback, WGS clientlatitude, longitudeThe native fields are not provided
Fallback, GCJ clientcorrected_latitude, corrected_longitudeThe native fields are not provided. The proto marks these "only for China cars"
Exceptiongeo_latitude, geo_longitudeRaw WGS GPS, usually less accurate. Use them when estimated_to_raw_distance (meters between the estimated and raw coordinates) shows a large gap, which mostly happens after the car was transported or towed. The proto gives no threshold

native_latitude_d, geo_latitude_d and the other double-precision variants are TESLEMETRY-EXT: do not use them.

A8.5 Not confirmed on a real car (#118)

  • The whole of getState: so far it has run only against FakeVehicle.
  • Which fields each category really carries, and how often they update.
  • The unit of speed and speed_float. Do not assume km/h or mph.
  • What timestamp marks: the measurement or the reply. Until that is known, the check in A8.3 is the best available.
  • How long a wake takes, and what a sleeping infotainment computer does with a request. The tests model no reply. If a real car answers busy instead, the library retries until the timeout, and the result can be Failure(Timeout(afterSend = false)) (16. Error Handling).
  • How the size limit behaves per firmware, and how the fault arrives on our own getState. In replies to another client it came in plaintext signedMessageStatus (Likely, 16, Response size limit).

A8.6 Wrapper fields: state, mode and status

Some fields that look like enums are small Wire messages whose members are all empty (Void) and belong to one oneof. Wire turns every member into a nullable property, and the one that is not null is the value. If the wrapper is null, or none of its members is set, the car did not send the value (or sent a member this library's schema does not know). Never compare a wrapper with a string or an enum value.

Field (category)Wire typeMembers (property names in Kotlin and Swift)Note
charge_state.charging_state (CHARGE)ChargeState.ChargingStateUnknown, Disconnected, NoPower, Starting, Charging, Complete, Stopped, CalibratingRead it after chargeStart and chargeStop (15. Commands)
charge_state.preconditioning_times, charge_state.off_peak_charging_times (CHARGE)PreconditioningTimes, OffPeakChargingTimesall_week, weekdaysThe days set by scheduleDeparture; how a policy that is off is reported is not confirmed
climate_state.climate_keeper_mode (CLIMATE)ClimateState.ClimateKeeperModeUnknown, Off, On, Dog, PartyThe command enum says CAMP; the state calls that member Party. That they are the same mode is not confirmed
climate_state.defrost_mode (CLIMATE)ClimateState.DefrostModeOff, Normal, Max
closures_state.sun_roof_state (CLOSURES)ClosuresState.SunRoofStateUnknown, Calibrating, Closed, Open, Moving, VentOnly set when the car has a sunroof
closures_state.sentry_mode_state (CLOSURES)ClosuresState.SentryModeStateOff, Idle, Armed, Aware, Panic, QuietOnly set where sentry mode is supported
software_update_state.status (SOFTWARE_UPDATE)SoftwareUpdateState.SoftwareUpdateStatusUnknown, Installing, Scheduled, Available, DownloadingWifiWait, Downloading
drive_state.shift_state (DRIVE)ShiftStateCarServer_Invalid (Invalid in the proto), P, R, N, D, SNA17. Platform Notes

Other value-like fields are plain nullable values that you read directly: for example charge_port_door_open (Boolean?), scheduled_charging_mode (the enum ChargeState.ScheduledChargingMode?), media_playback_status (the enum MediaPlaybackStatus?), and the seat_heater_* and seat_fan_front_* numbers (Int?: 0 off, 1 low, 2 medium, 3 high, as the proto's SeatHeaterLevel_E and SeatCoolingLevel_E list them).

import com.tesla.generated.carserver.vehicle.ChargeState
import com.tesla.generated.carserver.vehicle.ClimateState
import dev.mirolab.teslable.Vehicle
import dev.mirolab.teslable.model.StateCategory
import dev.mirolab.teslable.model.VehicleResult

// The member that is not null is the value. null (wrapper or every member) means the vehicle did not send it.
fun chargingStateName(state: ChargeState.ChargingState?): String? =
    when {
        state == null -> null
        state.Charging != null -> "charging"
        state.Starting != null -> "starting"
        state.Complete != null -> "complete"
        state.Stopped != null -> "stopped"
        state.Disconnected != null -> "disconnected"
        state.NoPower != null -> "no power"
        state.Calibrating != null -> "calibrating"
        state.Unknown != null -> "unknown"
        else -> null // no member set, or one this library's schema does not know
    }

suspend fun isCharging(vehicle: Vehicle): Boolean? {
    val result = vehicle.getState(StateCategory.CHARGE)
    if (result !is VehicleResult.Success) return null // keep the last value (16-lib-errors.md §getState results)
    val charging = result.value.charge_state?.charging_state ?: return null // not sent
    return charging.Charging != null
}

fun climateKeeperOn(mode: ClimateState.ClimateKeeperMode?): Boolean? =
    when {
        mode == null -> null
        mode.Off != null -> false
        mode.On != null || mode.Dog != null || mode.Party != null -> true
        else -> null // Unknown, or no member set
    }

Swift sees the wrapper as a class with optional members of the same names. This form passes a Swift typecheck against the framework; it has not run in a Swift program.

func chargingStateName(_ state: ChargeState.ChargingState?) -> String? {
    guard let state = state else { return nil }
    if state.Charging != nil { return "charging" }
    if state.Starting != nil { return "starting" }
    if state.Complete != nil { return "complete" }
    if state.Stopped != nil { return "stopped" }
    if state.Disconnected != nil { return "disconnected" }
    if state.NoPower != nil { return "no power" }
    if state.Calibrating != nil { return "calibrating" }
    if state.Unknown != nil { return "unknown" }
    return nil // no member set, or one this library's schema does not know
}

func isCharging(vehicle: Vehicle) async throws -> Bool? {
    let result = try await vehicle.getState(category: .charge, timeout: nil)
    guard case .success(let s) = onEnum(of: result), let data = s.value as? VehicleData else { return nil }
    guard let charging = data.charge_state?.charging_state else { return nil } // not sent
    return charging.Charging != nil
}

func climateKeeperOn(_ mode: ClimateState.ClimateKeeperMode?) -> Bool? {
    guard let mode = mode else { return nil }
    if mode.Off != nil { return false }
    if mode.On != nil || mode.Dog != nil || mode.Party != nil { return true }
    return nil // Unknown, or no member set
}

Result types

Field of VehicleStatusValues
lockStateUNLOCKED, LOCKED, INTERNAL_LOCKED, SELECTIVE_UNLOCKED, UNKNOWN
closuresEight openings, each a ClosureState; all CLOSED if the car did not send them (it omits them when everything is closed, like the Go getters)
sleepStatusAWAKE, ASLEEP, UNKNOWN
userPresencePRESENT, NOT_PRESENT, UNKNOWN
tonneauPercentOpenCybertruck only; null otherwise
gearPARK, REVERSE, NEUTRAL, DRIVE, UNKNOWN. UNKNOWN also when the car did not send it (proto3 cannot tell that apart from GEAR_UNKNOWN). A Teslemetry extension field with no Go counterpart; it is the last constructor parameter. Confirmed in broadcasts; Likely in query replies

The eight openings are frontDriverDoor, frontPassengerDoor, rearDriverDoor, rearPassengerDoor, rearTrunk, frontTrunk, chargePort and tonneau. Each is CLOSED, OPEN, AJAR, OPENING, CLOSING, FAILED_UNLATCH or UNKNOWN. uiDesire is not in this model: its meaning is not established, so it is not planned (the observation log prints the raw value).

Unknown values do not fail

When newer car firmware sends a value the library does not know yet, the field reads UNKNOWN instead of failing the query.

TypeFieldMeaning
WhitelistSummarynumberOfEntriesHow many keys are enrolled
slotMask, occupiedSlotsWhich slots are used, as a bit mask and as a sorted list

Connection and session state

These are properties of Vehicle that you observe, not questions sent to the car:

PropertyValues
connectionConnected, or Disconnected with the reason if known
sessionsFor VCSEC and INFOTAINMENT: None, Handshaking or Ready (with fromCache)
hasKeyWhether the connection was made with a key
vinThe VIN. Printing it shows a masked form; vin.value is the raw VIN, which should not go into logs

events is not a state but the stream of the car's broadcasts; see B2.

Sleep and wake-up

  • None of the queries wakes the car. Use sleepStatus to show whether it is asleep.
  • getState(category) needs an infotainment session and an awake car (A8.2). On a sleeping car it ends with Uncertain(Timeout(afterSend = true)).
  • wakeUp() (M4) needs only the VCSEC session. The call order with getState is in A8.2.
  • When a lock or trunk command (M4) comes back Uncertain, read vehicleStatus() again instead of sending the command a second time.

B1. What a broadcast is on the wire

A broadcast arrives on the same 00000213-... subscription as replies. It is a RoutableMessage with these properties:

FieldValue
from_destination.domainDOMAIN_VEHICLE_SECURITY (2)
to_destination.domainDOMAIN_BROADCAST (0), no routing address
request_uuidEmpty
signature_dataAbsent (plaintext, not authenticated)
protobuf_message_as_bytesSerialized FromVCSECMessage
#Field of FromVCSECMessage (6 to 10 reserved)TypeNotes
1vehicleStatusVehicleStatusSame message as the query reply (A2)
3authenticationRequestAuthenticationRequestTeslemetry extension
4commandStatusCommandStatusReplies to commands
16whitelistInfoWhitelistInfoQuery reply
17whitelistEntryInfoWhitelistEntryInfoQuery reply
46nominalErrorErrors.NominalErrorError reply
44, 48, 53unknownSeen on a real car, in no schema (B3)

B2. Broadcast events: vehicle.events

Reply matching works as before and as in the Go original: a broadcast has no routing address, so the dispatcher drops it from routing (drop reason TO_DOMAIN). A broadcast never reaches a pending request and cannot be mistaken for a reply. The observation log still writes its [drop:to-domain] line (18. Troubleshooting).

In addition, the library hands every plain broadcast to your app through vehicle.events (FR-035). The Go original has nothing like it; it just drops the frame. Kotlin and Swift examples are in 12. Getting Started with the Library; what each message means is in B3.

val events: Flow<VehicleEvent>                    // member of Vehicle

sealed interface VehicleEvent {                   // dev.mirolab.teslable.model
    val raw: ByteArray                            // the whole received frame; a new copy on every access; not VIN-masked
    val unknownFields: List<String>               // paths of fields the schema does not know, for example "53", "1.8"
    class Status(val status: VehicleStatus, …) : VehicleEvent
    class AuthenticationRequest(val requestedLevel: AuthenticationLevel, val reasons: List<AuthenticationReason>, …) : VehicleEvent
    class Unrecognized(…) : VehicleEvent
}

Which frames become events

All of the following must hold. It is the observation log's gate for its detailed decode, except that the payload does not have to decode (a payload that does not decode gives Unrecognized).

FieldRequired value
from_destinationdomain = DOMAIN_VEHICLE_SECURITY
to_destinationdomain = DOMAIN_BROADCAST (no routing address)
request_uuidEmpty
signature_dataAbsent
session_infoAbsent

Everything else stays an observation log line only: replies to other phones, signed or encrypted frames, frames that do not parse, frames without a source (including bare frames, B3.2). The library never decrypts a broadcast, and nothing it decrypted ever reaches an event (FR-112).

Event types

EventWhenWhat it carries
VehicleEvent.StatusThe frame has vehicleStatus (field 1)status: a VehicleStatus built with the same rules as vehicleStatus() (A2): no opening reported means all closed, unknown values become UNKNOWN, gear included
VehicleEvent.AuthenticationRequestThe frame has authenticationRequest (field 3)requestedLevel and reasons (B3.2). The 20-byte token is not a field; it is in raw
VehicleEvent.UnrecognizedNeither: only unknown fields (44, 48 and 53 on the real car), another field, no payload, or a payload that does not decode (then unknownFields is empty)Only raw and unknownFields
  • One frame can give two events. With both fields, Status comes first and AuthenticationRequest second, with the same raw and the same unknownFields. The schema puts the two fields in one oneof, so an ordinary decode would keep only one of them; the library reads both.
  • raw is the whole frame as received (without the 2-byte BLE length prefix), exactly as the car sent it in plaintext. Each access makes a new copy, so read it once. It is not VIN-masked: mask the VIN before you store or share it. Like the observation log's detailed decode, this is a narrow exception to "no plaintext payloads" that does not touch FR-112: only frames the car sent as plaintext, never anything the library decrypted.
  • Two events are equal when their fields and their raw bytes are equal. Printing an event shows only the length of raw.

unknownFields

Each entry is a path of field numbers from FromVCSECMessage down to a field the schema does not know:

PathMeaning
"53", "48"Top-level fields, in arrival order
"1.8"Field 8 inside vehicleStatus
"1.1.n", "1.5.n"Inside closureStatuses and inside detailedClosureStatus
"3.n", "3.2.n"Inside authenticationRequest and inside its token

An enum value outside the schema is listed under its field number too (the model shows UNKNOWN); a new gear value gives "1.7". Inside a sub-message its own fields come before those of its nested messages. A number that occurs twice is listed twice. A non-empty list means part of the frame was not understood; the bytes are in raw.

Rules for your app

  • A repeat is not a change. With nothing changing, the car resends its status 60.0 seconds after the previous Status (Likely; measured only on an awake car with a person present, B3.1), and several frames can come within one second around a change, some of them identical. A frame can also differ only in field 8, which the model does not show, so it reads as an unchanged VehicleStatus. The library does not filter repeats; compare with the previous value yourself.
  • A quiet minute is normal. Status frames can be 60 seconds apart, so up to 60 seconds without one says nothing about the link. Judge the link from connection, not from the time since the last event.
  • Trunk: opening and closing the trunk by hand from outside sent broadcasts (rearTrunk = OPEN, then no closureStatuses). With the interior button it sent none, so an app that tracks the trunk from events keeps seeing it CLOSED. When the trunk state matters, confirm it with vehicleStatus().
  • Waking car: the first frames of a sleeping car can show UNKNOWN closures and gear = UNKNOWN; real values follow within about 0.5 s (B3.1). Do not treat those UNKNOWN values as a change. The lock state reads UNLOCKED in them too, and since UNLOCKED is never encoded you cannot tell whether the car was still locked; confirm with vehicleStatus() if it matters.
  • gear = UNKNOWN on an awake car (Likely): twice a parked, awake car with a person present sent a 24-byte frame with no gear field, and a normal PARK frame followed within 0.1 s. Do not treat that UNKNOWN as a change.
  • Subscribing: a collection receives from the moment it starts; earlier broadcasts are not replayed. Several collectors each receive every event.
  • The end: the flow ends normally, without an exception, when connection becomes Disconnected for any reason or when you call disconnect(), whichever comes first. A collection started later ends at once. A frame that was still on its way at that moment can be lost. Decide about reconnecting from connection, and still call disconnect() after a link loss. A new connection is a new Vehicle with its own events.
  • Slow collector: the library keeps up to 64 frames for collectors that fall behind. Beyond that, new frames are dropped for every collector of the connection, counted, and logged as a WARN line on the first drop and every 100th. This is not an error (16. Error Handling). Keep the collector light.
  • No key and no session are needed; connect(vin, key = null) is enough. The real-car observation used a key that was not enrolled.

B3. Message catalogue

Status labels: Confirmed seen on a real car; Likely consistent and repeated in real-car logs, but not reproduced in a controlled scenario; Schema-only defined in the proto files, never seen by us; Unknown seen on the wire, meaning not known.

MessageStatusIn vehicle.events

Replies for other clients (not broadcasts)Confirmed for the signed VCSEC reply, Likely for the othersNot an event

B3.1 vehicleStatus

The fields are those of A2. What the real-car observation showed (iPhone, Model Y; first round 71 lines, second round one recording per scenario, third round one 40-second scenario of 25 lines starting from a sleeping car):

FieldStatusObserved
vehicleLockState (2)ConfirmedUNLOCKED right after connecting; INTERNAL_LOCKED repeated while seated with doors closed; LOCKED after getting out; UNLOCKED to LOCKED when walking away with the connection still up (link lost about 30 s later). Around driving, no scenario given (Likely, B5): INTERNAL_LOCKED became UNLOCKED in the same frame as PARK (3 of 3). It is not simply "in gear": after a shift to DRIVE it took 16.9 to 130.4 s to appear (3 cases), it did not appear in one 44-second stretch of REVERSE and DRIVE, and it ended once in DRIVE just as a door opened
vehicleSleepStatus (3)ConfirmedRounds 1 and 2: AWAKE in all lines. Round 3: the first two frames were ASLEEP when the exterior handle of the sleeping car was pulled, then AWAKE about 0.1 s later
userPresence (4)ConfirmedPRESENT while seated, NOT_PRESENT after getting out. Also PRESENT while a person stood outside with a door open (about 2.6 s after the rear door opened; about 0.1 s after the driver door opened from outside), NOT_PRESENT again about 0.2 s after the rear door closed. So PRESENT does not mean "seated"
gear (7)ConfirmedEach P/R/N/D change produced exactly one broadcast whose gear matched the order of operation (GEAR_REVERSE, GEAR_NEUTRAL, GEAR_DRIVE, GEAR_PARK, twice in a row), about 10 s apart as operated. It works without an infotainment session and with an unenrolled key
closureStatuses (1)Confirmed for front driver door, rear driver door, rear trunk and charge port; Likely for front passenger door, rear passenger door and front trunkPresent only while some field is not CLOSED, absent when all are CLOSED (the frame sizes match proto3, which never encodes a zero value). Driver door opened with the interior handle (round 2) or the exterior handle (round 3): frontDriverDoor = OPEN; absent again after closing. Rear driver door opened from outside: rearDriverDoor = OPEN. Rear trunk opened and closed by hand from outside: rearTrunk = OPEN, then absent. Charge port: chargePort = OPEN when opened (round 2) and when the latch was released by holding a door handle with a charger plugged in (round 3); absent again after unplugging and the port door closing. With the charger plugged in and latched it read CLOSED, so CLOSED does not mean "no cable". Trunk operated by the interior button: no broadcast at all. Waking car: the first frames had UNKNOWN (value 3) for the four doors and both trunks; frontTrunk stayed UNKNOWN for one more frame. With no scenario given (Likely, B5): frontPassengerDoor, rearPassengerDoor and frontTrunk each read OPEN and were absent again later; how they were opened was not recorded. Not tested: trunk by key card, phone key or app
uiDesire (6)Schema-onlyMeaning not established
detailedClosureStatus (5)Schema-onlyCybertruck tonneau; no data from a Model Y
field 8 (not in the schema)Unknown{1: 1|2, 2: 1|2, 3: 1|2}, in all 176 Status frames of the later recordings (B5). Meaning unknown. It is not a charging state: sub-field 2 was 1 in all 75 frames in DRIVE, REVERSE or NEUTRAL, where no cable can be in use, so the charge port changes of rounds 2 and 3 (B4) do not define it. Sub-field 1 changes at irregular gaps, once 7 times in about 8 s. Of the 111 frames with sub-field 1 = 2, 108 were PRESENT, yet 17 frames with sub-field 1 = 1 were PRESENT too, so presence does not define it either. Sub-field 3 is not always 1: it was 2 in 7 frames, parked with nobody present, flipping every 8 to 16 s. A change of field 8 alone produces a broadcast (29 of 32 changes). Not related to lights or turn signals

Field 8 values in the later recordings (176 frames, 32 value changes; the 2 frames with no gear field are in no column):

{1, 2, 3}DRIVE, REVERSE, NEUTRALPARK, PRESENTPARK, NOT_PRESENT
2, 1, 175282
1, 1, 101633
1, 2, 1018
2, 2, 1031
1, 1, 2007

Triggers seen: gear change, lock change (interior lock while seated, walk-away lock), door open and close with the interior and exterior handles (front and rear driver doors), trunk open and close by hand from outside, charge port open, latch release and close, charging start, change of user presence, waking up, a change of field 8 alone (Likely).

Frequency: a frame comes at each change, and it is also repeated with no change.

  • Repeat (Likely): on an awake car with a person present and nothing changing, the next Status came 60.0 s after the previous one: 47 gaps of 59.9 to 60.15 s (44 in DRIVE, 3 in PARK). No gap between two Status frames on one connection was longer (167 gaps, later recordings, B5).
  • What restarts the wait (Likely): a frame with a change. 6 of the 47 gaps started at one. Frames other than Status did not restart it (3 gaps, with field 53, whitelistInfo or commandStatus in between).
  • Counterexample: one gap between identical frames was 55.7 s, unexplained.
  • Not measured: a sleeping car, and an awake car with nobody present. With NOT_PRESENT something kept changing (the largest of 42 gaps was 25.3 s), so no repeat was seen in that state.
  • Earlier rounds: round 1 reported every 1 to 25 s while awake (26 bytes when UNLOCKED, 28 bytes otherwise). Those frames are not available to check. Field 8 changes may explain it: in the later recordings 37 of the 38 gaps of 5 to 59 s ended at a frame that changed something. Round 2: sometimes several within 1 s. Round 3: nine frames within 0.5 s while waking, then one every 0.1 to 8 s while things changed.

Do not treat a repeat as a change.

Library rule: a missing closureStatuses means all closed (A2), confirmed for both driver doors, the rear trunk and the charge port. It is wrong only for a trunk operated with the interior button, which sends no broadcast. UNLOCKED is the zero value of the lock state and is never encoded, so in a broadcast it cannot be told apart from a missing lock state (seen while the car was still ASLEEP).

B3.2 authenticationRequest (field 3)

Status: Confirmed to occur; the sub-field names are unverified (Teslemetry schema).

#Field (AuthenticationRequest)TypeMeaning
2sessionInfo (name unverified)AuthenticationRequestToken{token = 1}token: 20 bytes
3requestedLevel (name unverified)AuthenticationLevel_E0 NONE, 1 UNLOCK, 2 DRIVE (prefix AUTHENTICATION_LEVEL_)
4reasonsForAuth (name unverified)repeated AuthenticationReason_EWhy the car asks

AuthenticationReason_E (prefix AUTHENTICATIONREASON_): 0 NOT_DOCUMENTED, 1 IDENTIFICATION, 2 POWER_ON_VEHICLE_REQUEST, 3 GTW_REQUEST, 4 UI_UNLOCK_PASSIVE_AUTH, 5 PASSIVE_UNLOCK_EXTERIOR_HANDLE_PULL, 6 PASSIVE_UNLOCK_INTERIOR_HANDLE_PULL, 7 PASSIVE_UNLOCK_AUTOPRESENT_DOOR, 8 ENTERED_HIGHER_AUTH_ZONE, 9 WALK_UP_UNLOCK, 10 IMMOBILIZER. The related AuthenticationResponse (UnsignedMessage field 3, sent by a phone key) and AuthenticationRejection_E exist in the schema; the library does not send them.

In the public model (VehicleEvent.AuthenticationRequest, B2), requestedLevel is NONE, UNLOCK, DRIVE, or UNKNOWN for a value outside the schema; an absent level cannot be told from NONE. reasons has one entry per value sent, named as above without the prefix; a value outside the schema becomes UNKNOWN and is listed after the known ones (the exact order is in raw).

Observed: requestedLevel = DRIVE with reasons = [PASSIVE_UNLOCK_INTERIOR_HANDLE_PULL] when the interior handle was pulled; ENTERED_HIGHER_AUTH_ZONE was added when the charge port was opened and charging started. It came together with a vehicleStatus (and field 53) at the same moment. In the later recordings (B5) all 6 wrapped frames had requestedLevel = DRIVE, reasons = [ENTERED_HIGHER_AUTH_ZONE], and one bare frame had IDENTIFICATION (below). Other reasons were not observed. In round 3 (exterior handles on a sleeping, then awake car; charge port latch release; trunk by hand) no authenticationRequest and no field 53 arrived; PASSIVE_UNLOCK_EXTERIOR_HANDLE_PULL was never seen.

Usually wrapped, sometimes bare

This car normally sends it inside a RoutableMessage (the broadcast form above). Bare frames do occur (Likely): 2 in the 12 connections of the later recordings (B5), both without from_destination and read as a bare FromVCSECMessage. One is a 31-byte authenticationRequest{requestedLevel = DRIVE, reasons = [IDENTIFICATION], token = 20 B}, as a slow connect completed (B3.6). The other is a 12-byte commandStatus{OPERATIONSTATUS_OK}. That is far from the "about every 1.1 s" reported elsewhere (Teslemetry PR #179). These frames do not meet the conditions for an event (B2), so vehicle.events does not deliver them and an app cannot see this AuthenticationRequest. Only the observation log shows them, as [drop:missing-source] with reinterpreted= (18. Troubleshooting). drop:unparseable: 0 lines.

B3.3 Field 53 (unknown)

Status: Unknown. FromVCSECMessage field 53: one message with repeated sub-field 1, each entry {1: small int, 2: 35 bytes}.

  • Size by entries: 54 bytes for 1 entry (ids 3, 4 and 7 seen), 95 bytes for 2 entries (ids 3 and 6), 205 to 220 bytes for 5 entries (ids 1, 3, 4, 6, 7). The later recordings (B5) have 11 frames, four of them with 5 entries, all 220 bytes and all in the order 1, 3, 6, 7, 4, which is the field 48 value byte for byte (B3.4).
  • The ids are not key slots (Likely). They overlap with slot numbers, but keySummary reported only slots 0 to 6 in all 37 polls of a session in which id 7 appeared.
  • The 35-byte blob: the first 33 bytes were identical in all 28 entries of those 11 frames, on two dates and while the number of enrolled keys changed. Only the last 2 bytes vary, per entry, even for the same id (28 values in 28 entries).
  • When it comes: rounds 1 and 2 saw it with authentication events. In the later recordings only 1 of the 11 frames came within 3 s of an authenticationRequest. Three of the four 5-entry frames came within 0.7 s of field 8 sub-field 1 changing from 2 to 1, and the fourth 0.4 s after a slow connect.
  • The earlier guess of a per-key authentication challenge is weaker now: at most 16 bits differ between entries. Its meaning is unknown.

B3.4 Field 48 (unknown)

Status: Unknown. FromVCSECMessage field 48: {1: 5 bytes}, in a 20-byte frame. All 3 frames of the later recordings (B5) carry 01 03 06 07 04, the id order of the 5-entry field 53 frames (B3.3).

Rate: round 1 saw it about every 5.0 s while the car was awake and a person was present (with gaps), and round 2 did not see it. The later recordings have 3 frames in about 68 minutes connected, with no visible trigger. That does not fit a steady heartbeat, the earlier guess. The condition is unknown.

B3.5 Replies addressed to other clients

These are not broadcasts. Replies to another Bluetooth client reach our link too: to_destination.routing_address is that client's 16-byte address, and the library drops them as no-handler (owner other). They do not meet the conditions for an event (B2), so they are never events. Counts are from the later recordings (B5).

ReplyDomainFrameStatusSeen
Signed, empty body, 16-byte request_uuidVCSEC83 BConfirmedAbout every 1 to 2 minutes and right after a door event; 41 frames
Other VCSEC replies: encrypted with empty request_uuid, plaintext whitelistInfo and whitelistEntryInfo, session_infoVCSEC80 to 177 BLikely178 frames
Encrypted reply, 16-byte request_uuidInfotainment150 to 453 B; ciphertext up to 350 BLikely1685 frames
session_infoInfotainment179 and 180 BLikely66 frames
MESSAGEFAULT_ERROR_RESPONSE_MTU_EXCEEDED in signedMessageStatus, no payloadInfotainment106 BLikely132 frames (16, Response size limit)
  • The infotainment frames came at about one per second while that client was active (median gap 0.94 s). They appeared on 7 of the 12 connections. One 17-minute connection, mostly in DRIVE, carried none, so driving alone does not produce them.
  • The logs do not identify the client.
  • Queries can stall while this traffic runs (16. Error Handling).

B3.6 Field 44 (unknown)

Status: Unknown for the meaning, Likely for the link with slow connects. FromVCSECMessage field 44: a varint, value 2 in all 4 frames seen, in a 13-byte frame. Alone it is an Unrecognized event with "44" in unknownFields.

All 12 connections of the later recordings (B5) split cleanly by how long connecting took:

Connectionsconnecting to connectedWith a field 44 frameWith another client's infotainment frames
8 fast0.10 to 0.21 s0 of 87 of 8
4 slow1.52 to 13.58 s4 of 4, 1.1 to 3.4 s after connected0 of 4
  • The shortest fast connection lasted 26.7 s, so a frame at that delay would have been seen.
  • It is not simply "car locked": one fast connect to a locked car with nobody present had no field 44.
  • Why a connect is slow or fast, and what the value means, are unknown.

B4. What is not available by broadcast

ItemResult
Turn signals (left, right), hazards, headlights, high beamsNo message at all when operated, and no BLE schema field for them (vcsec.proto, vehicle.proto, car_server.proto). The only known source is Tesla Fleet Telemetry (LightsTurnSignal 240, LightsHazardsActive 239, LightsHighBeams 241), an internet server path outside this library's no-server scope
Trunk opened with the interior buttonNo broadcast. Opened and closed by hand from outside: broadcast (B3.1). Key card, phone key and app not tested
Sleeping carOnly its first frames while waking (round 3, ASLEEP for about 0.1 s). A car that stays asleep was not observed
Charging cableRound 2 (charge port open, charger connected, charging start): chargePort = OPEN, field 8 sub-field 2 changed 2 to 1, authenticationRequest with ENTERED_HIGHER_AUTH_ZONE, field 53 with one entry. Round 3 (latch release, unplug, port door closes): chargePort CLOSED while plugged in, OPEN after the latch release, absent after the port door closed; field 8 sub-field 2 changed 1 to 2. No separate broadcast for the cable itself was identified, and field 8 is not one (B3.1)
Another phone key approachingNot recorded

B5. Observation method and environment

  • iPhone 16 Pro, iOS 27, Model Y (firmware not recorded). Sample dev.mirolab.TeslableSampleDevice builds 7 and 8, key SECURE_ENCLAVE. The key does not need to be enrolled to observe.
  • First round 2026-10-18 (build 7, one continuous recording, 71 lines). Second round 2026-10-24 (build 8, detailed unknown-field decoding, one recording per scenario). Third round 2026-10-24 (build 8, one 40-second recording of 25 lines: sleeping, locked car; rear driver door opened from outside; charge port latch released while holding the handle; charger unplugged; rear door closed; charge port door closed; trunk opened and closed by hand from outside; driver door opened and closed from outside).
  • Later recordings: six recordings of the Monitor app on 2026-10-31 and 2026-11-01 (builds 13, 14 and 18), 12 connections, about 68 minutes connected, on the phone and car above. No scenario was given, so what a person did is inferred from the frames and these findings stay Likely or Unknown.
  • To reproduce: turn on the sample's observation mode, perform the scenario, then press the show button (and "copy whole result"). Details and log syntax: 18. Troubleshooting.

B6. Provenance

  • Official vehicle-command at a4b43c1 (Apache-2.0): routing, framing, VCSEC queries.
  • Teslemetry tesla-protocol at e6f7afb1 (Apache-2.0): proto snapshot with the extension fields (gear, uiDesire, authenticationRequest, extra enum values); ADR-0002 (revised 2026-10-18).
  • Teslemetry python-tesla-fleet-api PRs #178 and #179: facts only.
  • Our own captures: the real-car results note docs/handoff/2026-10-18-m4-push-observation-results.md and the log analysis docs/analysis/2026-11-01-build18-link-health.md, rechecked against the raw logs for this page.
  • AGPL repositories were not used.

15. Commands

A Vehicle can start and end sessions, send the twelve security-controller commands (locks, trunks, wake-up) and send the 51 infotainment commands: climate, charging and power, windows and sunroof, horn and lights, sentry and valet mode, media, software updates and ping(). This page lists all of them. Reading infotainment state with getState() is available too: see 14. Reading Vehicle State.

On this page
  1. Available today
  2. How every command behaves
  3. Security controller (M4)
  4. Infotainment (M5)
  5. Later

Available today

CallWhat it does
startSession(domains, timeout)Runs the handshake for VCSEC and Infotainment (both by default) in parallel. A domain already restored from the session cache succeeds without asking the car. Needs a key that is enrolled. If the phone lock blocks the key (an iOS Secure Enclave key that only works unlocked), it fails as soon as the car answers with KeyStoreError.Locked and is not retried (13). Default timeout 20 seconds
disconnect()Saves the session cache, erases session keys and closes the link. Safe to call more than once

While startSession runs, vehicle.sessions shows Handshaking; afterwards Ready, or None again if it failed or was cancelled.

How every command behaves

  • Commands are encrypted and authenticated with AES-GCM over the domain's session and expire after 5 seconds.
  • No command starts a session or wakes the car. Without a session for its part of the car, a command returns Failure(NoSession) and sends nothing.
  • Temporary failures are retried every second automatically. A command that may already have run is never sent again.
  • When a Bluetooth write fails and the library writes the same bytes again, the command is never signed again: an error that would mean a retry, or a refusal of the message itself (for example KeyNotPaired), is not taken as the result, and the call returns Uncertain(Timeout) unless an answer that settles it arrives in time (the late answer to the first copy, or the repeated copy's success when the first never arrived). See 16. Error Handling.
  • Security-controller commands run one at a time per connection; infotainment commands may run side by side.
  • An Uncertain result means: read the state, do not resend. See 16. Error Handling.

Security controller (M4)

All twelve methods are authenticated and need a VCSEC session; without one they return Failure(NoSession), so run startSession(setOf(VehicleDomain.VCSEC)) first. Each takes an optional timeout (default 5 seconds) and returns VehicleResult<Unit>.

MethodRequirementPayload (hex)Note
lock()FR-0401001Lock
unlock()FR-0411000Unlock
wakeUp()FR-042101eNeeds only the VCSEC session
remoteDrive()FR-0431014Remote drive
autoSecureVehicle()FR-044101dModel X
openTrunk()FR-04522022801Same as actuateTrunk(): a toggle
actuateTrunk()FR-04522022801Toggle
closeTrunk()FR-04522022804Powered trunks only
openFrunk()FR-04622023001There is no remote close
openTonneau()FR-04722024003Cybertruck
closeTonneau()FR-04722024004Cybertruck
stopTonneau()FR-04722024002Cybertruck
Key enrollment and removalFR-022–027See 13. Keys and Pairing
  • Likely failures: NoSession, NotConnected, InsufficientPrivileges (for example a Vehicle Monitor key, or the car state forbids it), VcsecRejected, UnknownVcsecError.
  • Uncertain means the car may have acted, also when a write failed and the library wrote the same bytes again. Do not resend; read vehicleStatus() for the lock state and closures. This matters most for the toggles openTrunk() and actuateTrunk(). See 16. Error Handling.
  • sessions follows the real session state. When a command ends with NoSession, or a session event arrives, the library re-checks that domain. A cached session the car no longer accepts (for example the cache belonged to another key) shows None after the first command fails; startSession recovers it.

Infotainment (M5)

All 51 methods (climate 12, charging and power 19, body 5, security 4, media 8, software update 2, and ping()) are authenticated over the infotainment session, take an optional timeout (default 5 seconds) and return VehicleResult<Unit>. The car's answer to a success carries nothing the app needs, as in the Go original. The commands are checked with automated tests against a simulated car; none has been checked on a real car yet (#118).

Before you send

  1. Start an infotainment session: startSession(setOf(VehicleDomain.INFOTAINMENT)), or both domains. Without it, a command returns Failure(NoSession) and sends nothing.
  2. Make sure the car is awake. The library does not wake it: if infotainment is asleep, the command goes out, nothing comes back, and the result is Uncertain(Timeout). Read vehicleStatus() first (no session needed, it never wakes the car). If it says ASLEEP, wake the car as in 14, A8.2 (VCSEC session, wakeUp(), wait for AWAKE) and start the infotainment session.
  3. Send the command and look at the result:
ResultMeaningWhat your app does
SuccessThe car accepted the commandTo show the new state, read it with getState (After Uncertain)
UncertainThe car may have done it: no answer in time (infotainment asleep, answer lost), a failed write the library repeated, or an unreadable answerNever send it again. Read the state with getState and decide from what it shows. Low power mode, accessory power, the horn and the lights cannot be read back
Failure(InvalidArgument)A check before sending failed, and nothing was sentFix the argument (next section)
Failure(InfotainmentRejected)The car refusedShow a generic message. reason is the car's own text; do not log it
NoSession, NotConnected, other errorsCalled in the wrong order, or the link is goneSee 16. Error Handling

Checks before sending

The library checks only what the Go original checks. The checks run before the session check, so a bad argument is reported as InvalidArgument even without a session, and nothing is sent.

MethodRuleMessage (detail)
setSeatCoolerFront seats only: FRONT_LEFT or FRONT_RIGHTinvalid seat position for seat cooler: <SEAT>
scheduleDepartureThe departure time is 0 to 1440 minutes after midnight, both ends includedinvalid departure time: <n> minutes after midnight (expected 0..1440)
enableValetModeThe PIN is exactly four ASCII digits 0–9. Digits from other scripts and full-width digits are refusedPIN codes must be four digits (the Go original's message; the PIN itself never appears in an error or a log)
setVolumeThe volume is 0 to 10, both ends included, and not NaNinvalid volume (should be in [0, 10]) (the Go original's message; the Go original lets NaN through, this library refuses it)

Everything else goes to the car as given, and the car decides: temperatures, charge limit, current, the other times, the schedule fields, the sunroof position and the software update delay. autoSeatAndClimate quietly leaves out the seats the Go original does not handle.

Argument types

TypeValuesUsed by
SeatPositionUNKNOWN, FRONT_LEFT, FRONT_RIGHT, SECOND_ROW_LEFT, SECOND_ROW_LEFT_BACK, SECOND_ROW_CENTER, SECOND_ROW_RIGHT, SECOND_ROW_RIGHT_BACK, THIRD_ROW_LEFT, THIRD_ROW_RIGHT, in the Go order. _BACK is the backrest, heated on some Model S onlySeat heater (every seat), seat cooler (front seats), auto seat climate (UNKNOWN and the front seats)
SeatClimateLevelOFF, LOW, MED, HIGHSeat heater, seat cooler
ClimateKeeperModeOFF, ON, DOG, CAMPClimate keeper
CabinOverheatProtectionTemperatureUNSPECIFIED, LOW, MEDIUM, HIGHCabin overheat protection temperature
ChargingPolicyOFF (that part is not sent), ALL_DAYS, WEEKDAYSScheduled departure
ChargeSchedule, PreconditionScheduleGenerated protocol types, sent as given. The id is a Long holding an unsigned 64-bit number; an id above Long.MAX_VALUE is a negative LongAdding charge and precondition schedules

The other commands take plain values: level: Int (sunroof), enabled: Boolean, pin: String, volume: Float and delaySeconds: Int.

All times are minutes after midnight in the car's local time. days_of_week is a bit mask; the Go command-line tool uses Sunday 1, Monday 2, Tuesday 4, Wednesday 8, Thursday 16, Friday 32 and Saturday 64 (every day 127, weekdays 62), and takes the current Unix time as a new schedule's id.

Ping

MethodRequirementExample payload (hex)Note
ping()FR-0341205f202020801ping_id 1, as in the Go original. What the car puts in its answer is not checked

ping() changes nothing and reads nothing. Success means the car knows the key and infotainment answered, which makes it a cheap check before a series of commands. It needs the same infotainment session and awake car as every command; Uncertain(Timeout) means no answer (asleep or lost). Because it changes nothing, your app may call it again after Uncertain; the library never resends it by itself. To read data, use getState.

After Uncertain

Never resend: read the state the command changes. The field names come from the official protocol definitions; which field a command changes on a real car is not confirmed yet (#118). The read itself can come back Uncertain (infotainment asleep, answer too large); then the command's result stays unknown until a later read succeeds.

CommandsRead withLook at
climateOn, climateOffgetState(StateCategory.CLIMATE)climate_state.is_climate_on
setClimateTempsamedriver_temp_setting, passenger_temp_setting
setSeatHeater, setSeatCoolersameseat_heater_*, seat_fan_front_left and _right
setSteeringWheelHeater, autoSeatAndClimatesamesteering_wheel_heater, auto_seat_climate_left and _right
setClimateKeeperMode, setBioweaponDefenseModesameclimate_keeper_mode, bioweapon_mode_on (cars with a HEPA filter)
setCabinOverheatProtection, setCabinOverheatProtectionTemperaturesamecabin_overheat_protection, cop_activation_temperature
setPreconditioningMaxsameThe climate state; no single field is identified
chargeStart, chargeStop, chargeStandardRange, chargeMaxRangegetState(StateCategory.CHARGE)charge_state.charging_state, charge_limit_soc
