pingNoneNothing changed; call ping() again if needed

Some of these fields are small wrapper messages, not enums or numbers: charging_state, off_peak_charging_times, climate_keeper_mode, sun_roof_state, sentry_mode_state and status. How to read them in Kotlin and Swift: 14, A8.6.

Kotlin example

import com.tesla.generated.carserver.common.ChargeSchedule
import com.tesla.generated.carserver.common.MediaPlaybackStatus
import dev.mirolab.teslable.Vehicle
import dev.mirolab.teslable.model.ChargingPolicy
import dev.mirolab.teslable.model.SeatClimateLevel
import dev.mirolab.teslable.model.SeatPosition
import dev.mirolab.teslable.model.StateCategory
import dev.mirolab.teslable.model.VehicleError
import dev.mirolab.teslable.model.VehicleResult

// Before: an INFOTAINMENT session (startSession) and an awake vehicle (14-lib-reading-state.md A8.2).
suspend fun warmUp(vehicle: Vehicle): String {
    when (val result = vehicle.climateOn()) {
        is VehicleResult.Success -> Unit
        is VehicleResult.Uncertain -> {
            // The vehicle may have turned climate on. Never resend: read the state.
            val state = vehicle.getState(StateCategory.CLIMATE)
            val on = (state as? VehicleResult.Success)?.value?.climate_state?.is_climate_on
            return if (on == true) "climate on" else "unknown, read again later"
        }
        is VehicleResult.Failure -> return "not sent or refused: ${result.error.message}"
    }
    // Handle these results the same way.
    vehicle.setClimateTemp(driverCelsius = 22.0f, passengerCelsius = 21.5f)
    vehicle.setSeatHeater(mapOf(SeatPosition.FRONT_LEFT to SeatClimateLevel.HIGH, SeatPosition.FRONT_RIGHT to SeatClimateLevel.LOW))
    return "climate on"
}

suspend fun planCharging(vehicle: Vehicle, scheduleId: Long, latitude: Float, longitude: Float): String {
    // Leave at 08:00, off-peak charging ends at 06:00 (minutes after the vehicle's local midnight).
    when (val departure = vehicle.scheduleDeparture(480, 360, ChargingPolicy.ALL_DAYS, ChargingPolicy.WEEKDAYS)) {
        is VehicleResult.Success -> Unit
        is VehicleResult.Uncertain -> {
            // The vehicle may have stored it. Never resend: read the state and stop here.
            val state = vehicle.getState(StateCategory.CHARGE)
            val departAt = (state as? VehicleResult.Success)?.value?.charge_state?.scheduled_departure_time_minutes
            return if (departAt == 480) "departure set, schedule not added" else "unknown, read again later"
        }
        is VehicleResult.Failure ->
            return if (departure.error is VehicleError.InvalidArgument) {
                "departure time outside 0..1440 minutes; nothing was sent"
            } else {
                "not sent or refused: ${departure.error.message}"
            }
    }
    val schedule =
        ChargeSchedule(
            id = scheduleId, // the Go CLI uses the current Unix time
            name = "Home",
            days_of_week = 127, // every day
            start_enabled = true,
            start_time = 120, // 02:00
            enabled = true,
            latitude = latitude,
            longitude = longitude,
        )
    return when (val added = vehicle.addChargeSchedule(schedule)) {
        is VehicleResult.Success -> "departure and schedule set"
        is VehicleResult.Uncertain -> {
            // Never resend: look for the id in the charge schedules.
            val state = vehicle.getState(StateCategory.CHARGE_SCHEDULE)
            val schedules = (state as? VehicleResult.Success)?.value?.charge_schedule_state?.charge_schedules.orEmpty()
            if (schedules.any { it.id == scheduleId }) "departure and schedule set" else "schedule unknown, read again later"
        }
        is VehicleResult.Failure -> "schedule not sent or refused: ${added.error.message}"
    }
}

suspend fun togglePlayback(vehicle: Vehicle): String =
    when (val result = vehicle.toggleMediaPlayback()) {
        is VehicleResult.Success -> "toggled"
        is VehicleResult.Uncertain -> {
            // A toggle run twice ends where it started. Never resend: read the state and let the user decide.
            val state = vehicle.getState(StateCategory.MEDIA)
            when ((state as? VehicleResult.Success)?.value?.media_state?.media_playback_status) {
                MediaPlaybackStatus.Playing -> "playing"
                MediaPlaybackStatus.Paused, MediaPlaybackStatus.Stopped -> "not playing"
                null -> "unknown, read again later"
            }
        }
        is VehicleResult.Failure -> "not sent or refused: ${result.error.message}"
    }

suspend fun enterValetMode(vehicle: Vehicle, pin: String): String =
    when (val result = vehicle.enableValetMode(pin)) {
        is VehicleResult.Success -> "valet mode on"
        is VehicleResult.Uncertain -> {
            // The vehicle may have turned it on. Never resend: read the state.
            val state = vehicle.getState(StateCategory.CLOSURES)
            val on = (state as? VehicleResult.Success)?.value?.closures_state?.valet_mode
            if (on == true) "valet mode on" else "unknown, read again later"
        }
        is VehicleResult.Failure ->
            if (result.error is VehicleError.InvalidArgument) {
                "the PIN must be four digits" // the error never contains the PIN; do not log the PIN
            } else {
                "not sent or refused: ${result.error.message}"
            }
    }

Swift example

Swift passes every argument, including timeout:. Schedules are built with every field plus unknownFields; the details are in 17. Platform Notes. This form passes a Swift typecheck against the framework; it has not run in a Swift program.

func warmUp(vehicle: Vehicle) async throws -> String {
    let result = try await vehicle.climateOn(timeout: nil)
    switch onEnum(of: result) {
    case .success:
        break
    case .uncertain:
        // The vehicle may have turned climate on. Never resend: read the state.
        let state = try await vehicle.getState(category: .climate, timeout: nil)
        if case .success(let s) = onEnum(of: state), let data = s.value as? VehicleData,
           data.climate_state?.is_climate_on?.boolValue == true {
            return "climate on"
        }
        return "unknown, read again later"
    case .failure(let f):
        return "not sent or refused: \(f.error.message)"
    }
    _ = try await vehicle.setClimateTemp(driverCelsius: 22.0, passengerCelsius: 21.5, timeout: nil)
    _ = try await vehicle.setSeatHeater(levels: [.frontLeft: .high, .frontRight: .low], timeout: nil)
    return "climate on"
}

func planCharging(vehicle: Vehicle, scheduleId: Int64, latitude: Float, longitude: Float) async throws -> String {
    // Leave at 08:00, off-peak charging ends at 06:00 (minutes after the vehicle's local midnight).
    let departure = try await vehicle.scheduleDeparture(
        departAtMinutes: 480, offPeakEndMinutes: 360, preconditioning: .allDays, offPeak: .weekdays, timeout: nil)
    switch onEnum(of: departure) {
    case .success:
        break
    case .uncertain:
        // The vehicle may have stored it. Never resend: read the state and stop here.
        let state = try await vehicle.getState(category: .charge, timeout: nil)
        if case .success(let s) = onEnum(of: state), let data = s.value as? VehicleData,
           data.charge_state?.scheduled_departure_time_minutes?.int32Value == 480 {
            return "departure set, schedule not added"
        }
        return "unknown, read again later"
    case .failure(let f):
        if case .invalidArgument = onEnum(of: f.error) {
            return "departure time outside 0..1440 minutes; nothing was sent"
        }
        return "not sent or refused: \(f.error.message)"
    }
    let schedule = ChargeSchedule(
        id: scheduleId, name: "Home", days_of_week: 127, start_enabled: true, start_time: 120,
        end_enabled: false, end_time: 0, one_time: false, enabled: true,
        latitude: latitude, longitude: longitude, unknownFields: OkioByteString.companion.EMPTY)
    let added = try await vehicle.addChargeSchedule(schedule: schedule, timeout: nil)
    switch onEnum(of: added) {
    case .success:
        return "departure and schedule set"
    case .uncertain:
        // Never resend: look for the id in the charge schedules.
        let state = try await vehicle.getState(category: .chargeSchedule, timeout: nil)
        if case .success(let s) = onEnum(of: state), let data = s.value as? VehicleData,
           (data.charge_schedule_state?.charge_schedules ?? []).contains(where: { $0.id == scheduleId }) {
            return "departure and schedule set"
        }
        return "schedule unknown, read again later"
    case .failure(let f):
        return "schedule not sent or refused: \(f.error.message)"
    }
}

func togglePlayback(vehicle: Vehicle) async throws -> String {
    let result = try await vehicle.toggleMediaPlayback(timeout: nil)
    switch onEnum(of: result) {
    case .success:
        return "toggled"
    case .uncertain:
        // A toggle run twice ends where it started. Never resend: read the state and let the user decide.
        let state = try await vehicle.getState(category: .media, timeout: nil)
        guard case .success(let s) = onEnum(of: state), let data = s.value as? VehicleData,
              let status = data.media_state?.media_playback_status else { return "unknown, read again later" }
        return status == .playing ? "playing" : "not playing"
    case .failure(let f):
        return "not sent or refused: \(f.error.message)"
    }
}

func enterValetMode(vehicle: Vehicle, pin: String) async throws -> String {
    let result = try await vehicle.enableValetMode(pin: pin, timeout: nil)
    switch onEnum(of: result) {
    case .success:
        return "valet mode on"
    case .uncertain:
        // The vehicle may have turned it on. Never resend: read the state.
        let state = try await vehicle.getState(category: .closures, timeout: nil)
        if case .success(let s) = onEnum(of: state), let data = s.value as? VehicleData,
           data.closures_state?.valet_mode?.boolValue == true {
            return "valet mode on"
        }
        return "unknown, read again later"
    case .failure(let f):
        if case .invalidArgument = onEnum(of: f.error) {
            return "the PIN must be four digits" // the error never contains the PIN; do not log the PIN
        }
        return "not sent or refused: \(f.error.message)"
    }
}

Later

Planned for v1.1: HomeLink, clearing PIN to Drive, speed limit mode, parental controls, guest mode, renaming the car and nearby chargers. Setting PIN to Drive is not possible over BLE and is left out. The Go equivalents of every command are listed in 7. Command Reference.

16. Error Handling

Every call returns one of three outcomes, and every error says whether the car may have acted and whether trying again could help. The library already retries what is safe to retry, so most apps only need to branch on the outcome.

On this page
  1. Three outcomes
  2. Error types
  3. Invalid arguments
  4. getState results
  5. Bluetooth and key store errors
  6. Broadcast events
  7. Retries and timeouts
  8. What to read after Uncertain
  9. Queries while another Bluetooth client is active

Three outcomes

OutcomeMeaningWhat your app does
SuccessThe car confirmedUse the value
UncertainA command: the car may have done it, but no confirmation arrived. A query: no data was confirmed, and nothing was done (getState results)A command: do not send it again, read the state instead (vehicleStatus(), or getState() for infotainment state). A query: keep the last value and ask again later
FailureIt did not happenLook at error

In Swift, switch onEnum(of: result) covers .success, .uncertain and .failure; see 12. Getting Started. Cancelling your own coroutine or Task is not turned into an outcome; the cancellation propagates as usual.

Error types

Every VehicleError carries a message (English), mayHaveSucceeded and temporary. shouldRetry() is true when the error is temporary and the car cannot have acted. The library already retries those, so you do not need a retry loop. One exception: KeyStoreError.Locked is temporary, but the library never retries it, because only the user can unlock the phone; call again after the unlock.

TypeWhenTemporaryMay have succeeded
ProtocolFaultThe car sent a protocol fault codeOnly for busy, timeout, signature, counter, internal, epoch and expiry faultsOnly for NONE and RESPONSE_MTU_EXCEEDED
KeyNotPairedThe car does not know this keynono
InsufficientPrivilegesThe key role or the car state (driving, valet) does not allow the commandnono
BusyThe car asked to waityesno
UnknownFault, UnknownKeychainCode, UnknownVcsecError, UnknownResponseNewer firmware sent a code that is not in this library's protocol definitions; check for an updatenono
NotConnected, NoSession, RequiresKeyCalled in the wrong order or without a keynono
BadResponseThe answer could not be readnoyes for VCSEC and Infotainment answers
KeychainRejected, VcsecRejected, InfotainmentRejectedThe car understood but refusednono
TransportErrorBluetooth scan, connect or writeOnly scan timeout, connect failed, write failedno
KeyStoreErrorPlatform key store; Locked also from startSession()Only Lockedno
TimeoutThe command ran out of timeyesIf its first Bluetooth write had already started (Retries and timeouts); never for the handshake
InvalidArgumentA bad argument; nothing was sent (Invalid arguments)nono

The meaning of every protocol fault code is in 9. Errors and Troubleshooting.

Codes from the extended protocol definitions

Since 2026-10-18 the library's protocol definitions come from a pinned snapshot of Teslemetry/tesla-protocol, which adds codes to the official ones (see 19. Architecture). A code in that snapshot is now reported with its named error type instead of an Unknown… type with a number. Whether the library retries does not change. The code names come from the snapshot and are not confirmed on a real car.

CodeBeforeNow
Protocol fault 29 MESSAGEFAULT_ERROR_COMMAND_REQUIRES_PHYSICAL_PROXIMITYUnknownFault(29)ProtocolFault, not retried
Key list codes 29 to 32 (rider and predelivery keys)UnknownKeychainCodeKeychainRejected
VCSEC errors 8 GENERICERROR_KEY_NOT_FOUND and 9 GENERICERROR_NOT_SUPPORTEDUnknownVcsecErrorVcsecRejected
Session info status 2 SESSION_INFO_STATUS_INVALID_HANDLEUnknownResponseUnknownResponse (the official Go code does not know it either)

Check the error type, not message: the message now shows the code name instead of the number.

Invalid arguments

InvalidArgument means the library refused an argument before sending anything. Nothing reached the car, so there is nothing to read back: fix the argument. The library checks only what the Go original checks; other values go to the car as given and the car decides (for example a charge limit or a temperature out of range comes back as InfotainmentRejected, or succeeds).

CallRuleMessage (detail)When it is checked
setSeatCooler(level, seat)Front seats only: FRONT_LEFT or FRONT_RIGHTinvalid seat position for seat cooler: <SEAT>Before the session check, so it is returned even without a session
scheduleDeparture(departAtMinutes, …)The departure time is 0 to 1440 minutes after midnight, both ends includedinvalid departure time: <n> minutes after midnight (expected 0..1440)Before the session check
enableValetMode(pin)The PIN is exactly four ASCII digits 0–9; digits from other scripts and full-width digits are refusedPIN codes must be four digits (the PIN itself never appears in an error or a log)Before the session check
setVolume(volume)The volume is 0 to 10, both ends included, and not NaNinvalid volume (should be in [0, 10])Before the session check
addKey, addKeyRequest (as PairingEvent.Failed)The role is not KeyRole.UNKNOWNrole UNKNOWN cannot be enrolledBefore sending (13. Keys and Pairing)
connect(vin, key, target)The target advertisement belongs to vinadvertisement <local name> is not for this VINBefore connecting
Any requestThe framed message is at most 1024 bytes; a very long schedule name could go over itmessage exceeds 1024 bytesBy the Bluetooth layer, after the session check

The Go original returns invalid seat position and invalid departure time for the first two. The rule and the moment of the check are the same; only the message says more here. The PIN and volume messages are the Go original's own. The Go original lets a NaN volume through (both of its comparisons are false); this library refuses it. Check the error type, not detail: the text is for people and logs.

getState results

A query changes nothing on the car. So Uncertain on a read means no data was confirmed, not that a command may have run. mayHaveSucceeded is still true, because reads and commands share the error types. There is nothing to check afterwards: keep the last value and ask again later. Asking again is safe, but not in a tight loop; the library sets no interval, so it is your app's choice.

ResultMeaningWhat your app does
Success(data)The car answeredCheck every field you use for null. data can be empty, and a success is not proof of a new measurement: compare timestamp with the previous reply (14, A8.3)
Uncertain(ProtocolFault(RESPONSE_MTU_EXCEEDED))The car received the request but the reply was too large. The car is reachableKeep the last value with its age; see the next section
Uncertain(Timeout(afterSend = true))Nothing came back in time after the request's write started: infotainment is asleep, the reply was lost, the link dropped while waiting, or the phone did not finish the write in time (app in the background)Read vehicleStatus(). ASLEEP: your app may wake the car (14, A8.2). Otherwise keep the last value with its age. Check connection too
Uncertain(BadResponse(detail))A reply arrived but could not be readKeep the last value. Report detail if it repeats
Failure(Timeout(afterSend = false))Time ran out before the request's write started or while waiting to retry, for example when the car keeps answering busy while it wakesAsk again later, with a longer timeout right after a wake
Failure(NoSession)No infotainment session (nothing was sent), or the car did not accept the saved onestartSession(setOf(VehicleDomain.INFOTAINMENT)), then ask again once
Failure(InfotainmentRejected(reason))The car refused. This wins over any data in the replyShow a generic message. reason is the car's own text; do not log it
Failure(NotConnected), TransportErrorThe link is gone or the write failedCall disconnect(), then connect again (below)

Any other error means what the error type table above says. Your interface can tell the two main Uncertain cases apart by error type: a size fault means the car answered, a timeout means nothing came back. Neither is a reason to clear the session.

Response size limit on getState

The car sends no data when its reply goes over a size limit. The largest reply seen on real cars is about 453 bytes; this is an observed value, not a specification. getState() returns Uncertain(ProtocolFault(RESPONSE_MTU_EXCEEDED)), and the app cannot make the reply smaller.

It is most likely on Drive. A long destination name makes it more frequent, with reports of failures lasting tens of seconds, and of one Model S/X build (2026.27.6.15, South Korea) failing almost always even with no route.

The library does not resend the request. It does not clear the session, reconnect or wake the car because of this fault; session information that came with the reply is still applied as usual. The fault does not mean the car is gone or asleep. Keep the last value with its age, show it as unavailable when it is too old, and do not retry in a tight loop. Live speed is not guaranteed, and how this behaves per firmware is not confirmed on a real car (#118).

Real-car evidence (Likely, not from our own getState): in one session replies to another Bluetooth client reached our link (14, B3.5), and 132 of its 1690 infotainment frames carried MESSAGEFAULT_ERROR_RESPONSE_MTU_EXCEEDED. They were 106-byte frames with no payload and the fault in plaintext signedMessageStatus. All 132 fell within about 11 minutes of one connection, in runs of one or two, and most runs were followed at once by a session_info reply; the next connection had none. The largest frame in that session was 453 bytes (350 bytes of ciphertext). What the client asked for is unknown: its requests are not visible and the payloads are encrypted.

Reply authenticity

Do not base safety decisions on getState

Like the Go original, the library accepts a plaintext reply, because firmware before 2024.38 does not encrypt responses (vehicle-command/pkg/protocol/protocol.md:751). A plaintext reply carries no authentication tag, so a party on the BLE link could forge read data. The same goes for vehicleStatus() and vehicle.events, which are unauthenticated plaintext by design (14, A1, B1). Command replies are not exempt: the plaintext path has no domain check, so a forged plaintext VCSEC reply can make a command such as lock() return Success even if the car never ran it. Planned, not implemented yet (ADR-0015): authenticated commands will need a verified encrypted reply to succeed, and firmware before 2024.38 will no longer be supported.

Bluetooth and key store errors

Connecting is already retried every second until the connect timeout. The last column is what the app should do.

ErrorMeaningWhat to do
ScanTimeoutNo advertisement of the car was found before the connect deadline. A connection that found the car, or started opening a target the library remembers, never ends with this errorCheck the car is in range, try again
MaxConnectionsExceededThe car already has three Bluetooth connectionsDisconnect another phone or app, try again
ConnectFailedConnecting, discovering services or subscribing failed, or the connect deadline passed after the car was found or while opening a remembered targetTry again in a moment
DisconnectedThe link droppedCall disconnect(), then connect again
BluetoothOffBluetooth is offAsk the user to turn it on
PermissionDeniedNo Bluetooth permissionAsk for permission or open Settings
WriteFailedSending failedRetried automatically; reconnect if it keeps happening
HardwareUnavailableHardware was required but is not availableUse PREFER_HARDWARE
KeyNotFoundNo key under that aliasCreate one with getOrCreate
PlatformFailureThe platform key store failed; stored key is corrupted means the saved key is damagedDamaged keys are not replaced automatically: delete, create, and enroll the new key again
LockediOS: the phone is locked and the key only works while the phone is unlocked, or the phone has not been unlocked since it restarted. A key that cannot be created for this reason is not replaced by a software key. startSession() also returns it when the lock blocks a Secure Enclave key read before the phone lockedDo not delete or re-create the key: try again after the phone is unlocked. To use the key while the phone is locked, create an AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK key under a new alias and enroll it (13. Keys and Pairing). The library retries neither key store calls nor the handshake

Which error a connect deadline gives. The error of the last failed attempt comes first. If no attempt failed, there are three cases. If the library's own scan saw the car (the observation log has a [connect:scan-hit] line), it is ConnectFailed, and the text says the car was found. If the library was opening a target it remembers from your app's scan, it is also ConnectFailed, but no scan ran and that advertisement can be stale, so the text does not say the car was found. A remembered target that is out of range reports ConnectFailed, not ScanTimeout. If no advertisement was found, it is ScanTimeout. Before #140 these were all ScanTimeout. These errors are temporary and nothing was executed, so retrying works the same way; only code that looks at the error type sees a difference. The text in ConnectFailed.cause is for people and logs, so do not match on it. The Go original does not separate these cases: it returns one generic deadline error.

Broadcast events

vehicle.events has no error values and never throws. It ends normally when the link drops or disconnect() is called (14. Reading Vehicle State, B2); why the link dropped is in connection.

SituationWhat happensWhat to do
A collector falls more than 64 frames behindNew frames are dropped for every collector of the connection and counted. A WARN line (tag Dispatcher) on the first drop and every 100th: Dropping plain broadcast because the broadcast buffer is full (dropped N since connect). Not an error: no VehicleError, and the flow keeps runningKeep the collector light and hand slow work to another coroutine. The car resends its status, but that can take 60 s while awake with a person present and was not measured otherwise (Likely, 14, B3.1). To get the state sooner, read vehicleStatus()
A broadcast the library cannot decodeAn Unrecognized event with an empty unknownFields; the bytes are in rawKeep raw (with the VIN masked) for analysis

Retries and timeouts

The library retries with the same rules as the Go original, with two differences, both after a failed Bluetooth write: the Go original gives up after a failed write, while this library writes the same bytes again; and after such a write a command is never signed again, because the car may already have run the first copy.

SituationWhat the library does
A temporary error the car cannot have acted on (busy, wait, expired, bad counter…)Waits one second, signs the command again with a fresh counter and expiry, and resends it. Session information that came with the error is applied first. A command whose write failed does not retry (two rows down); the session information is still applied
A temporary Bluetooth write errorWrites the same bytes again after one second, without signing them again, until a write succeeds or time runs out
A command whose write failed, so the same bytes went out again, gets an error that would mean a retry, or any refusal of the message itself (any car error code except "reply too large", an unknown key, missing privileges)Does not sign a new copy. The answer may be about a duplicate of a copy the car already ran. The library logs a warning (possible duplicate) and keeps waiting on the same request. A success (usually the late answer to the first copy), a refusal by the command itself (security controller or infotainment), a reply that is too large and an unreadable answer end the call as usual; if none comes in time, the result is Uncertain(Timeout)
The same for a read (vehicleStatus(), keySummary(), listKeys(), getState())Retried as in the first row. A read changes nothing on the car, so running it twice is harmless
An error after which the car may have acted (no answer, unreadable answer)Never resends, so a toggle cannot run twice. Returns Uncertain
Time runs out before the request's first Bluetooth write starts: waiting for the previous security-controller command, saving the counter reservation in the session cache, waiting for the link to be free, or a check before writingFailure(Timeout)
Time runs out while waiting to retry after the car asked for a retry (busy, wait, expired…)Failure(Timeout)
Time runs out after the first Bluetooth write started: the write has not finished, it failed and the library is resending, or the answer has not comeUncertain(Timeout)
The handshake runs out of timeFailure(Timeout); no session was made and nothing changed
The phone lock blocks the key during the handshake (iOS)Failure(KeyStoreError.Locked) as soon as the car answers, not retried although it is temporary; no session. A saved session the lock kept from being restored stays saved
TeslaBleConfigDefaultUsed for
commandTimeout5 sEach query or command
commandLifetime5 sHow long the car accepts a signed command
handshakeTimeout20 sstartSession()
connectTimeout30 sScan and connect
pairingTimeout60 saddKeyRequest()

"Sent" means the write started (changed 2026-11-07). A command or query counts as possibly sent from the moment its first Bluetooth write starts, not when the phone confirms the write. A failed write does not undo this: the library sends the same bytes again, and a timeout during that resend is still Uncertain. Before this change, a timeout that came while the write was still running was reported as Failure(Timeout) ("timed out before sending") although the car may already have had the request. This happened once on a real car, with the iPhone app in the background: a listKeys() request ended after 9.65 seconds (the limit was 5), and the car's answer to it arrived 9 milliseconds later. The Go original never meets this case, because its Bluetooth write ignores the deadline. Nothing else changes for your app: Failure(Timeout) means nothing was executed, Uncertain(Timeout) means it may have been, so read the state before sending a command again. Expect fewer Failure(Timeout) and more Uncertain(Timeout) results when the phone is slow to finish writes, for example in the background.

No new copy after a failed write (changed 2026-11-08). A write that reports failure may still have reached the car. If the car ran that first copy and its answer was lost, the copy the library writes again carries the same counter, and the car refuses it as a replay. Before this change the library took that refusal as a reason to retry, signed a new copy with a new counter, and the car could run the command twice: a toggle such as openTrunk() would end where it started. Now a command whose write failed sends no new copy. It keeps listening on the same request until an answer settles the call (usually the late answer to the first copy), and otherwise returns Uncertain(Timeout) at the time limit. Your app does what it does for any Uncertain: read the state and do not resend. For the same reason, a refusal of the message such as KeyNotPaired or InsufficientPrivileges is not reported as Failure on this path: it may be about the duplicate (removing your own key, for example, makes the repeated copy's key unknown). Without a failed write nothing changes: when the car answers with an error and fresh session information, for example on the first request with a saved session that is out of step, the library applies it and retries as in the first row. The Go original never gets here, because it gives up on the failed write.

The saved counter is reserved before use (changed 2026-11-14, ADR-0016). Every signed request carries an anti-replay counter that the car accepts only once. Before a signed request uses a counter that the session cache does not hold yet, the library saves a reservation 16 counters ahead and only then signs and writes the request. That happens before the first signed request of a connection and then once every 16 requests; unsigned requests never save anything. For your app this changes three things:

  • When the app ended without disconnect(), the first request of the next launch is no longer refused once and resent about 1.2 s later (Troubleshooting lists what can still cause it).
  • Saving the reservation counts against the call's time limit. A timeout while saving is Failure(Timeout): nothing was sent and no counter was used. If the cache throws, the request is still sent and a warning is logged. The built-in caches never throw: they log a failed write (a warning with the tag cache) and the library counts it as saved for the next 16 requests. If the connection is closing, the call returns Failure(NotConnected) without sending.
  • A session that has used the last counter (0xFFFFFFFF; not expected in practice) returns Failure(NoSession) without sending: call startSession() and send again. The Go original keeps retrying until its time limit.

What to read after Uncertain

After Uncertain, never resend. Read the state the command changes:

CommandRead withLook at
lock, unlock, wakeUp, remoteDrive, autoSecureVehicle, openTrunk, actuateTrunk, closeTrunk, openFrunk, openTonneau, closeTonneau, stopTonneauvehicleStatus()Lock state, closures, sleep status. openTrunk and actuateTrunk toggle, so compare the closure state before deciding to send again
addKey, removeKeylistKeys()Whether the public key is in the car's key list
addKeyRequest (TimedOut, or the flow ended without a final answer)sessionInfo(publicKey, VehicleDomain.INFOTAINMENT)Success = enrolled; Failure(KeyNotPaired) = not (yet)
Climate commands: climateOn, climateOff, setClimateTemp, setSeatHeater, setSeatCooler, setSteeringWheelHeater, autoSeatAndClimate, setPreconditioningMax, setClimateKeeperMode, setBioweaponDefenseMode, setCabinOverheatProtection, setCabinOverheatProtectionTemperaturegetState(StateCategory.CLIMATE)The matching climate field (15. Commands)
Charging commands: chargeStart, chargeStop, setChargeLimit, setChargingAmps, chargeStandardRange, chargeMaxRange, scheduleCharging, scheduleDeparture, clearScheduledDeparture, openChargePort, closeChargePortgetState(StateCategory.CHARGE)The matching charging field

Body and security: ventWindows, closeWindows, setSunroof, setSentryMode, enableValetMode, disableValetMode, resetValetPingetState(StateCategory.CLOSURES)The window, sunroof, sentry or valet field (15. Commands)
Media: volumeUp, volumeDown, setVolume, mediaNextTrack, mediaPreviousTrack, mediaNextFavorite, mediaPreviousFavorite, toggleMediaPlaybackgetState(StateCategory.MEDIA)audio_volume, now_playing_title, media_playback_status. Never send toggleMediaPlayback again: sent twice, it ends where it started
scheduleSoftwareUpdate, cancelSoftwareUpdategetState(StateCategory.SOFTWARE_UPDATE)status, scheduled_time_ms
setLowPowerMode, setKeepAccessoryPowerMode, honkHorn, flashLightsNoneThe official vehicle data has no field for these. Show the result as unknown and do not resend
pingNoneIt changed nothing; your app may call it again

For infotainment the read can itself come back Uncertain (infotainment asleep, answer too large, getState results); then the command's result stays unknown until a later read succeeds. Which field a command changes on a real car is not confirmed yet (#118).

Queries while another Bluetooth client is active

Status: Likely, from real-car logs of one car (14, B3.5). It was not reproduced in a controlled scenario, and the mechanism is unknown.

While another Bluetooth client was using the car, its infotainment replies reached our link at about one frame per second and queries could get no reply at all:

SessionOther client's infotainment frameslistKeys()vehicleStatus(), keySummary()
With that traffic, 53 polls16906 Success, 47 Uncertain(Timeout(afterSend = true))106 of 106 Success
Without it, 37 polls037 of 37 Success74 of 74 Success
  • This is the ordinary lost-reply result: the request was sent and nothing came back. No late reply arrived after the timeout either, so a longer timeout is not expected to help.
  • listKeys() is the most exposed because it sends requests one after another, the summary plus one per occupied slot (eight requests in about 0.8 s with seven keys). Requests sent under 0.55 s after the other client's latest frame were all answered, and 45 of the 47 lost ones were sent 0.55 to 0.85 s after it. Send times are inferred from the previous reply, because the log has no transmit lines.
  • vehicleStatus() and keySummary() are not immune: earlier recordings with such traffic have 4 Uncertain(Timeout) results on them. Their causes are not established; one came 1.6 s before a link loss.
  • The link stayed up: the next poll's vehicleStatus() succeeded every time.
  • No command was sent during this traffic, so whether a command stalls the same way is unknown.
What your app doesWhy
Keep the last value with its age and ask again laterThe same rule as any Uncertain on a query. It can keep failing while the other client stays active (only 6 of 53 succeeded above), so do not retry in a tight loop
Do not read a timed-out listKeys() as a key changeNo data was confirmed. Only a Success shows the key list
Do not reconnect or clear the session because of itThe link is up. Check connection as usual
After Uncertain on a command, read the state and do not resendWhat to read after Uncertain. That rule does not change here

17. Platform Notes

What an Android or iOS app has to set up, what the iOS Swift package contains, where the library keeps keys and sessions, a few platform behaviours worth knowing, and how Swift calls the library. Items not yet confirmed on a real device say so.

On this page
  1. Support
  2. Android
  3. Android background use by the sample
  4. iOS
  5. iOS background use by the sample
  6. Key storage
  7. Session cache
  8. Clocks
  9. Connection slots and dropped links
  10. Swift: timeouts
  11. Swift: generated types (getState)
  12. Swift: command arguments (infotainment commands)

Support

  • Android 12 (API 31) or later, iOS 16 or later, with the app in the foreground. Background Bluetooth is out of scope.
  • The JVM target is for tests and experiments. Its keys live in memory and are gone when the process ends.

Android

Declare the two Bluetooth permissions and request them at runtime. Without them, scanning and connecting return PermissionDenied. No internet permission is needed: the library never calls a server.

<uses-permission android:name="android.permission.BLUETOOTH_SCAN" android:usesPermissionFlags="neverForLocation" />
<uses-permission android:name="android.permission.BLUETOOTH_CONNECT" />
<uses-feature android:name="android.hardware.bluetooth_le" android:required="true" />
  • Create the library with PlatformContext(context); it keeps the application context.
  • On connect the library asks for an MTU of 517 and falls back to 20-byte blocks if that fails.
  • Keystore entries are named teslable.key:<alias> and teslable.wrap:<alias>, so they do not collide with your app's own entries.
Exclude the key files from Auto Backup

A software key is saved as files/teslable/keys/<alias>.enc, wrapped by a Keystore key that is never backed up. If Auto Backup restores the file without its wrapping key, the key reads as corrupted. Exclude files/teslable/keys/ in dataExtractionRules or fullBackupContent. The sample app simply sets android:allowBackup="false". Saved sessions in files/teslable/sessions are harmless to lose, but excluding them is cleaner.

Android background use by the sample

The library is supported in the foreground (see Support). The Android sample's monitor app keeps its connection in the background with a foreground service and has not been checked on a real device yet, so treat the library's behaviour in the background as unconfirmed.

  • Manifest: FOREGROUND_SERVICE, FOREGROUND_SERVICE_CONNECTED_DEVICE, POST_NOTIFICATIONS (asked at runtime) and <service android:foregroundServiceType="connectedDevice" />. Still no INTERNET.
  • Android 14 and later: a connectedDevice foreground service can only start after BLUETOOTH_CONNECT is granted, and only while the app is visible (or another start exemption applies). Do not rely on the system to restart it.
  • The service owns the single library instance and calls vehicle.disconnect() after every link loss, then connects again with a new Vehicle (2 second wait, doubling to 30 seconds). Retrying is the app's job, not the library's.
  • Key store: the sample creates its key with KeyAccessibility.AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK; Android ignores the argument and reports AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK (per API, not yet confirmed on a device, see Key storage).

iOS

  • Add NSBluetoothAlwaysUsageDescription to Info.plist. The first scan shows the permission prompt; if the user declines, calls return PermissionDenied.
  • Create the library with PlatformContext().
  • The Swift package (Installation) contains a static Teslable.xcframework with exactly two slices: ios-arm64 for devices and ios-arm64-simulator for the simulator (arm64 only), for iOS 16.0 or later. Being static, the library code is linked into your app binary.
  • Xcode embeds a small Teslable.framework (about 33 KB) in YourApp.app/Frameworks. A Swift package needs no setting for this; it is what Embed & Sign does for a framework added by hand. Because the framework is static, Xcode takes the library out of the embedded copy and puts in an empty stub binary, so the bundle stays valid for the App Store. This is Xcode's intended behaviour for every static binary package: the library code is linked into your app binary, and the app never loads the stub. Do not remove it. App Store acceptance is expected but not yet verified by this project. Checked with Xcode 27.2.
  • No separate dSYM ships with the package. The Kotlin debug info is inside the static library, and your app's Release build copies it into the app's dSYM, so Kotlin frames in crash reports show file and line (inlined frames included) from the app's dSYM alone. Upload the app's dSYM as usual.
  • The simulator has no Bluetooth. Scanning and connecting can only be tried on a real iPhone. On the simulator, scan(vin) reports one ScanEvent.Failed right away with ConnectFailed and the reason IllegalStateException: This device doesn't support the Bluetooth low energy central or client role (seen with Xcode 27.2), not BluetoothOff or PermissionDenied. connect without a target fails the same way at once. The reason is text for people and logs: do not branch on it. The key store does work on the simulator, in a signed app (see Key storage).
  • iOS has no MTU negotiation API; the library uses the write length the system picks. The observed block length on a real iPhone is 512. The response characteristic supports both notifications and indications; the Kable default subscription (notify) was confirmed on a real car (ADR-0003).
  • Swift calls Kotlin through SKIE: suspend functions become async throws, Flow becomes AsyncSequence, sealed types work with switch onEnum(of:), and companion members are reached through .companion.
  • In Swift the public VehicleStatus is named VehicleStatus_, because a generated VCSEC message type already takes the plain name. Results of vehicleStatus() and VehicleEvent.Status.status have this type.
Simulator: Apple Silicon Macs only

The Swift package has no x86_64 simulator slice. An Intel Mac can build the app for a device but cannot run the library in the simulator. A simulator build that also builds x86_64 fails to link with ld: symbol(s) not found for architecture x86_64, after warnings that the Teslable files were built for arm64. A Release build for the simulator builds every architecture, so set EXCLUDED_ARCHS[sdk=iphonesimulator*] = x86_64 on the app target. Debug builds that build only the active architecture, the Xcode default, are not affected on an Apple Silicon Mac. Checked with Xcode 27.2.

iOS background use by the sample

The library is supported in the foreground (see Support). The iOS sample's monitor app declares UIBackgroundModes: [bluetooth-central] and runs the same reconnect loop as the Android sample. It has not been checked on a real iPhone yet, so treat the library's behaviour in the background as unconfirmed.

  • Info.plist (through project.yml): UIBackgroundModes: [bluetooth-central]; UIFileSharingEnabled and LSSupportsOpeningDocumentsInPlace only for the sample's log folder.
  • The app owns the single library instance for its lifetime and calls vehicle.disconnect() after every link loss, then connects again with a new Vehicle (2 second wait, doubling to 30 seconds). Retrying is the app's job, not the library's.
  • Unconfirmed: whether iOS lets the app scan and reconnect while it is in the background or the phone is locked (the library currently scans by device name, and iOS usually needs a service UUID filter for background scans; plan Tasks 2 and 8). Until the real-car spike runs, the only supported background case is a link that stays up: events keep arriving while iOS keeps the app running.
  • Key store: the sample creates its key with KeyAccessibility.AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK. While the phone is locked and the key store answers KeyStoreError.Locked, the sample waits, retries, and never deletes the key.

Key storage

protectionMeaning
SECURE_ENCLAVEiOS Secure Enclave. The private key never leaves the chip
STRONGBOXAndroid StrongBox security chip
TEEAndroid Trusted Execution Environment
SOFTWAREEmulators, simulators, or a device where every hardware attempt failed
  • Software keys are wrapped by an Android Keystore AES key, or stored as an iOS Keychain item that never leaves the device and is protected according to the key's accessibility setting.
  • On iOS, a failed Secure Enclave lookup is reported as an error, not as "no key", so a working key is never overwritten by mistake.
  • There is no API to export a private key.
  • Key handles are released automatically. On iOS each key object holds one Security framework reference (SecKeyRef). It is released once neither the app nor a connected Vehicle uses the key any more and the Kotlin/Native garbage collector has run. There is nothing to close, and reading the key again does not pile up handles.
  • On the simulator the Keychain works only in a code-signed app. Ad-hoc signing is enough (CODE_SIGN_IDENTITY = -, shown in Xcode as "Sign to Run Locally"). An unsigned build (CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED = NO) has no Keychain access, and the key store returns PlatformFailure with keychain status -34018 (errSecMissingEntitlement). The release check of the Swift package signs its simulator test this way.

Using the key while the phone is locked

Each key has an accessibility setting, VehicleKey.accessibility. How to choose it and switch keys is in 13. Keys and Pairing.

PlatformWhen a key is createdWhat an existing key reports
iOSWHEN_UNLOCKED (default) uses kSecAttrAccessibleWhenUnlockedThisDeviceOnly and AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK uses kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly, for the Secure Enclave key and the software key alikeThe setting read back from the stored key. It never changes afterwards
AndroidThe setting is ignoredAlways AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK: the Keystore keys have no unlock or user-authentication requirement, and the key file can be read after the first unlock. By the API; not yet confirmed on a device
JVMThe setting is ignoredAlways AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK: keys in memory have no lock
  • On a locked iPhone, the Keychain error errSecInteractionNotAllowed (-25308) becomes KeyStoreError.Locked. A WHEN_UNLOCKED key cannot be read or used for a handshake while the phone is locked; an AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK key can, once the phone was unlocked after the last restart. Before that first unlock every key is locked. A key that cannot be created because the phone is locked is not replaced by a software key.
  • A key object read before the phone locked: when the Secure Enclave refuses the key agreement with the same error (-25308), the handshake ends with KeyStoreError.Locked as soon as the car answers and is not retried, and a saved session that needed the key is not restored but stays saved (13). Other key agreement errors behave as before (the handshake keeps trying until its timeout). A software key object keeps its private key in memory and keeps working after the lock (per the API).
  • Not yet confirmed on a device: the Keychain round trip of both settings, reading the setting back, and a handshake on a locked iPhone with an AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK Secure Enclave key; also which error a locked iPhone reports for the key agreement of a WHEN_UNLOCKED Secure Enclave key (the library expects -25308; with another error startSession() still ends with a timeout). They are checked with the sample app (build 12 is ready; the checks are plan Task 11). Using the key while the phone is locked also needs the app to keep running in the background, which these pages do not cover yet.
A corrupted key is never replaced automatically

If getOrCreate or get returns PlatformFailure with stored key is corrupted, tell the user, then call delete and getOrCreate. The new key has a different public key and must be enrolled on the car again. Replacing it silently would leave the user unable to control the car without knowing why.

Session cache

  • Saved sessions let the next connection skip the handshake. They are stored in files/teslable/sessions/ on Android, in the Keychain on iOS, and in memory on the JVM. You can supply your own with TeslaBleConfig.sessionCache.
  • Names are hashes of the VIN, so the VIN itself is not stored.
  • Sessions belong to one key. After the key changes, or when the car has moved on, old sessions are discarded on the next connection.
  • A missing or outdated cache is not an error; the library simply starts a new session.
  • A saved session that could not be restored because the phone lock blocked the key (iOS) is not outdated. The library writes nothing to the cache until that part of the car has a session again, so disconnecting while the phone is locked keeps the saved sessions. Exception: if another saved session turned out to be outdated in the same connection, the library rewrites the cache without it, and the blocked session is dropped too (that part of the car makes a new handshake next time).
  • The app may end without disconnect(): the user force-quits it, the system ends it while suspended, or it crashes. On iOS this is how an app usually ends. The library saves the anti-replay counter before it uses it (a reservation 16 counters ahead, ADR-0016), so the first signed request of the next launch is accepted at once, without a handshake and without a refusal. Before this change (builds up to 2026-11-14) the car refused that request once with an invalid-counter fault and fresh session information; the library applied it and resent the request, which added about 1.2 s. The result was still Success and the refused copy did not run. A cache saved by such an older build can still cause this once after the upgrade.
  • The reservation costs one cache write (a file write on Android, a Keychain write on iOS) before the first signed request of each connection and then one every 16 requests. It counts against that call's time limit; a timeout there is Failure(Timeout) because nothing was sent.
  • This assumes that the cache write succeeds and that your app keeps one live connection per car and key. If a write fails, the library logs it, sends the request anyway, and the old behaviour can come back once. Two connections to the same car with the same key, or another app using the same key, are not protected. This includes a dropped link: call disconnect() on the old Vehicle and let it return before you connect again, because its final cache write could otherwise land after the new connection's reservation and lower it.

Clocks

Session timing uses the monotonic clock, which stops on some platforms while the phone sleeps. After a long sleep, the first command from a saved session may fail with an expiry fault. The library then takes the fresh session information the car sends back and retries; the app sees nothing.

Connection slots and dropped links

  • A car accepts at most three Bluetooth connections. Call disconnect() when you are done so the slot is free. When all three are taken, connecting returns MaxConnectionsExceeded.
  • When the link drops and connection turns Disconnected, still call disconnect() so the session keys are erased. Calling it again is harmless.
  • A disconnected Vehicle cannot be reused; connect again with TeslaBle.connect.

Swift: timeouts

Kotlin's Duration is an inline value class. Swift sees it only as a boxed Any?, so Swift code cannot build one directly. Two helpers solve this.

  • Timeouts.shared.seconds(seconds:) returns the boxed value to pass as any timeout: argument. Zero, negative, NaN or infinite input throws IllegalArgumentException. Timeouts.shared.millisecondsOf(timeout:) returns the milliseconds of a boxed value (-1 for nil); it exists to check the round trip. The iOS sample's XCTest target runs in tools/ci/verify.sh step (d).
  • TeslaBleConfig.companion.withSeconds(logger:commandTimeoutSeconds:handshakeTimeoutSeconds:connectTimeoutSeconds:pairingTimeoutSeconds:) builds a config from seconds. Every argument is required; commandLifetime keeps its default. TeslaBleConfig.companion.default(logger:) is unchanged.
  • timeout: nil still means "use the config default".

Swift: generated types (getState)

getState returns the Wire generated VehicleData, which behaves differently in Swift from the hand-written models. The full example is in 14. Reading Vehicle State. These forms pass a Swift typecheck against the framework; they have not run in a Swift program.

  • vehicle is the Vehicle protocol. Pass every argument: vehicle.getState(category: .drive, timeout: nil), or timeout: Timeouts.shared.seconds(seconds: 8). Without timeout: the compiler reports missing argument for parameter 'timeout' in call.
  • The generic of VehicleResult is erased, so s.value is AnyObject?. Cast it: s.value as? VehicleData.
  • Nullable numbers bridge as KotlinInt? and KotlinFloat?, not Int32? and Float?. Read .int32Value and .floatValue. let speed: Int32? = data.drive_state?.speed is rejected, and the compiler says only failed to produce diagnostic for expression, so that message will not help.
  • Generated fields keep their proto names in Swift (drive_state, speed_float, charge_state), unlike the camelCase hand-written models.
  • timestamp is a Wire Instant: charge.timestamp?.getEpochSecond() gives Int64?.
  • shift_state is not an enum. ShiftState is a Wire oneof message: its members P, R, N, D, SNA and CarServer_Invalid are nullable, and the one that is not nil is the gear (data.drive_state?.shift_state?.P != nil). If none is set, the vehicle sent no gear. Do not compare it with a string or an enum value.
  • Every other state oneof with Void members works the same way: charge_state?.charging_state, charge_port_latch, closures_state?.sun_roof_state, sentry_mode_state, software_update_state?.status, location_state?.native_type (GCJ or WGS) and climate_state?.climate_keeper_mode. The member that is not nil is the value (charging_state?.Charging != nil). A message that arrives with no member set reads the same as an absent one, so treat both as "no value". Fields of nested messages such as ParentalControlsSettings are KotlinBoolean? / KotlinFloat? / KotlinInt? like the top-level ones, while ChargeSchedule and PreconditionSchedule entries in the repeated lists have plain values (enabled, one_time), so an absent flag there reads as false.
  • The type names are plain: VehicleData, DriveState, StateCategory. Only VehicleStatus_ has the name clash.
  • StateCategory is a real Swift enum, so a switch over all 12 needs no default: .charge, .climate, .drive, .location, .closures, .chargeSchedule, .preconditioningSchedule, .tirePressure, .media, .mediaDetail, .softwareUpdate, .parentalControls.
  • The enum in .protocolFault is MessageFault_E. The size limit case is .messagefaultErrorResponseMtuExceeded: case .protocolFault(let p) where p.fault == .messagefaultErrorResponseMtuExceeded:.

Swift: command arguments (infotainment commands)

How Swift calls the infotainment commands (15. Commands). These forms pass a Swift typecheck against the framework (the debug simulator framework, 2026-11-08); they have not run in a Swift program.

  • Pass every argument, including timeout: (nil uses the config default): try await vehicle.climateOn(timeout: nil). Without it the compiler reports missing argument for parameter 'timeout' in call.
  • The argument enums are real Swift enums, so a switch over them needs no default: SeatPosition (.unknown, .frontLeft, .frontRight, .secondRowLeft, .secondRowLeftBack, .secondRowCenter, .secondRowRight, .secondRowRightBack, .thirdRowLeft, .thirdRowRight), SeatClimateLevel (.off, .low, .med, .high), ClimateKeeperMode (.off, .on, .dog, .camp), CabinOverheatProtectionTemperature (.unspecified, .low, .medium, .high), ChargingPolicy (.off, .allDays, .weekdays).
  • setSeatHeater(levels:timeout:) takes a Swift dictionary [SeatPosition: SeatClimateLevel], and a literal works: try await vehicle.setSeatHeater(levels: [.frontLeft: .high, .secondRowCenter: .off], timeout: nil). An empty [:] is sent too. Swift dictionaries have no order; the library sends the seats in the order of SeatPosition anyway.
  • autoSeatAndClimate(seats:enabled:timeout:) takes a Swift array: seats: [.frontLeft, .frontRight].
  • Numbers: percent, amps, the minutes, the sunroof level and delaySeconds are Int32, schedule ids Int64, temperatures and volume Float; plain number literals work, as in scheduleDeparture(departAtMinutes: 480, offPeakEndMinutes: 360, preconditioning: .allDays, offPeak: .weekdays, timeout: nil), setVolume(volume: 5.5, timeout: nil), setSunroof(level: 0, timeout: nil) and scheduleSoftwareUpdate(delaySeconds: 7200, timeout: nil).
  • The valet PIN is a Swift String: enableValetMode(pin: pin, timeout: nil). Do not log it.
  • After an uncertain toggleMediaPlayback(timeout:), read the playback state instead of sending it again: data.media_state?.media_playback_status == .playing (MediaPlaybackStatus is a Swift enum: .playing, .paused, .stopped).
  • ChargeSchedule and PreconditionSchedule are generated protocol classes. Swift gets no default arguments, so build them with every field plus unknownFields: OkioByteString.companion.EMPTY. ChargeSchedule() is rejected with 'init()' is unavailable. The fields keep their protocol names:
let schedule = ChargeSchedule(
    id: 1_700_000_000, name: "Home", days_of_week: 127, start_enabled: true, start_time: 120,
    end_enabled: false, end_time: 0, one_time: false, enabled: true,
    latitude: 0, longitude: 0, unknownFields: OkioByteString.companion.EMPTY)
_ = try await vehicle.addChargeSchedule(schedule: schedule, timeout: nil)
_ = try await vehicle.removeChargeSchedule(id: schedule.id, timeout: nil)   // id is Int64

let precondition = PreconditionSchedule(
    id: 2, name: "Work", days_of_week: 62, precondition_time: 450, one_time: false, enabled: true,
    latitude: 0, longitude: 0, unknownFields: OkioByteString.companion.EMPTY)
_ = try await vehicle.addPreconditionSchedule(schedule: precondition, timeout: nil)
  • An argument the library refuses comes back as .failure with .invalidArgument: if case .failure(let f) = onEnum(of: result), case .invalidArgument(let bad) = onEnum(of: f.error) { … bad.detail … } (16. Error Handling).

18. Troubleshooting

Common problems with scanning, connecting, keys, pairing, commands, getState and the iOS simulator, what usually causes them, and what to do. A development-only observation log is described at the end.

Scanning and connecting

SymptomLikely causeWhat to do
PermissionDeniedBluetooth permission missing or declinedAndroid: request BLUETOOTH_SCAN and BLUETOOTH_CONNECT at runtime. iOS: add NSBluetoothAlwaysUsageDescription; if declined, send the user to Settings
BluetoothOffBluetooth is offAsk the user to turn it on
The scan finds nothing, connecting ends with ScanTimeoutOut of range or wrong VINMove closer, check the VIN (the car is matched by a name derived from it)
On the iOS simulator, the scan reports ConnectFailed at once with the reason IllegalStateException: This device doesn't support the Bluetooth low energy central or client role, and connecting fails the same wayThe simulator has no Bluetooth, and iOS reports that the device cannot act as a Bluetooth central. It is not reported as BluetoothOff or PermissionDeniedUse a real iPhone for scanning and connecting. Do not tell the user that Bluetooth is off, and do not retry in a loop (Platform Notes)
Connecting ends with ConnectFailed, and the reason says the connection did not complete before the deadlinevehicle found in the reason: the library's own scan found the car (its [connect:scan-hit] line shows the discovery), but the link did not open in time. remembered target in the reason: no scan ran, so nothing shows the car is still there. A remembered target that is out of range reports ConnectFailed, not ScanTimeout. ScanTimeout always means no advertisement was foundTry again. After a remembered target, connect without it so that a scan runs. If it repeats while the car is in range, report the log lines
MaxConnectionsExceededThe car already has three Bluetooth connectionsDisconnect another phone or app, and always call disconnect() in yours
ConnectFailed keeps coming backWeak signal or a busy carThe library already retries for 30 seconds; try again later
connect returns some seconds after connectTimeout, for example at 32.6 seconds instead of 30Most likely a late timer: the app was probably not running when it was due (iOS suspends a background app). connect does not wait for a failed attempt's cleanupNothing to repair; see The connect time budget
connection turns DisconnectedThe link was lostCall disconnect(), then connect again

Keys and sessions

SymptomLikely causeWhat to do
startSession() returns RequiresKeyConnected without a keyConnect with a VehicleKey
startSession() returns KeyNotPairedThe car does not know this keyEnroll it with addKeyRequest (13. Keys and Pairing). Check with listKeys() or sessionInfo
stored key is corruptedAndroid backup restored the key file without its wrapping key, or storage damageDelete, create and enroll a new key; exclude the key files from backup (Platform Notes)
HardwareUnavailableHardware required on a device without StrongBox, TEE or Secure EnclaveUse PREFER_HARDWARE
KeyStoreError.LockediOS: the phone is locked and the key only works while the phone is unlocked (WHEN_UNLOCKED), or the phone has not been unlocked since it restartedDo not delete the key. Try again after the phone is unlocked. To use the key while the phone is locked, create an AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK key under a new alias and enroll it (13. Keys and Pairing)
startSession() returns KeyStoreError.Locked while the iPhone is locked, with a WARN line Session info not applied for <domain>: the private key is locked (device locked)The Secure Enclave key object was read before the phone locked and only works while the phone is unlocked, so the handshake cannot use itDo not delete the key. Call startSession() again after the phone is unlocked, with the same key. To use the key while the phone is locked, create an AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK key under a new alias and enroll it (13. Keys and Pairing)
Connecting while the iPhone is locked restores no saved session, with a WARN line Cached session for <domain> not restored: the private key is locked (device locked) and later an INFO line Keeping the session cache: …The same cause, while restoring the saved sessionNothing to repair: the saved session is kept and is restored on the first connection after the unlock
startSession() times out only while the iPhone is locked, with a WARN line Session info error: MESSAGEFAULT_ERROR_BAD_PARAMETER: invalid public key or invalid cache: …: invalid public keyiOS reported the locked Secure Enclave key with an error other than the one the library expects (-25308; not yet confirmed on a device)Please report it with the iOS version and the log lines. Meanwhile read the key from the key store right before connecting, which then returns Locked, or use an AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK key
WARN Keychain did not report the protection class of key <alias>; reporting WHEN_UNLOCKEDThe Keychain did not say how the stored key is protected (not expected)The key reports WHEN_UNLOCKED. Please report it with the iOS version
First command after a long sleep fails, the retry succeedsThe phone's clock paused while it sleptNothing; the library resynchronizes on its own
The first signed request after a relaunch is refused with INVALID_TOKEN_OR_COUNTER and fresh session information, then succeeds about 1.2 s laterThe saved counter was behind the car. Since ADR-0016 the library saves the counter before it uses it, so an app that ended without disconnect() (force quit, iOS ending a suspended app, a crash) no longer causes this. What can still cause it: a cache saved by an older build (once after the upgrade), a cache write that failed (the two rows below), another connection or app using the same keyNothing to repair: the refused copy did not run and the result is Success. Keep one live connection per car and key, and let disconnect() of a dropped connection return before connecting again. If it repeats, look for the warnings in the two rows below
A WARN line (tag Dispatcher) Sending on <domain> with a counter the session cache does not cover …, often after Failed to store the counter reservation for <domain>: … (tag VehicleSession) or Keeping the session cache: …The counter reservation could not be saved before the request (your own TeslaBleConfig.sessionCache threw, or the cache is being kept because the phone lock blocked a restore). The request was sent anywayNothing for this request. After the app ends without disconnect(), the next launch's first request may be refused once (row above). If saving keeps failing, check your TeslaBleConfig.sessionCache or the storage
A WARN line (tag cache) Failed to store session cache: …The built-in cache could not write (file system, Keychain status). It logs and returns, so the library counts the reservation as saved and the next 16 signed requests use counters the cache may not holdNothing for these requests. After the app ends without disconnect(), the next launch's first request may be refused once (two rows above). If it repeats, check free storage or the Keychain status in the line
Connecting restores no saved session for one part of the car, with a WARN line invalid cache: <domain>: anti-replay counter exhaustedThe saved counter has fewer than 16 values left before 0xFFFFFFFF (not expected in practice)Nothing: startSession() makes a handshake and starts from the car's counter
A signed call returns NoSession after a WARN line The anti-replay counter for <domain> is exhausted; startSession() starts a new sessionThe session used the last counter, 0xFFFFFFFF (not expected in practice)Call startSession() for that part of the car, then send again: nothing was sent

Pairing and commands

SymptomLikely causeWhat to do
addKeyRequest ends with TimedOutThe card was not tapped, the on-screen confirmation was not given in time (60 seconds by default), or the reply was not recognizedAsk the user to tap the key card on the center console and confirm on the screen, then check sessionInfo(publicKey, INFOTAINMENT): the key may still have been enrolled
addKeyRequest emits Rejected(KeychainRejected(WHITELIST_FULL)) or another KeychainRejected codeThe car refused: its key list is full, no permission to add…Remove an unused key on the car's touchscreen or with removeKey; see 16. Error Handling
addKeyRequest emits Unknown(raw)A reply format not seen before; real-car behaviour is not verified yet (ADR-0009)Report raw as hex (no VIN) and what the car did

If sessions showed Ready(fromCache = true), it changes to None after this failure (the cache belonged to another key or car); startSession fixes it

Reading state with getState

SymptomLikely causeWhat to do
getState() returns NoSessionNo infotainment session yet, or the saved session is staleCall startSession(setOf(VehicleDomain.INFOTAINMENT)), then ask again. If sessions showed Ready(fromCache = true), it changes to None after this failure
getState() returns Uncertain(Timeout(afterSend = true)) although startSession succeededInfotainment is asleep (a saved session says nothing about sleep, and getState never wakes the car), or the reply was lostRead vehicleStatus(). ASLEEP: follow the wake order in 14, A8.2. Otherwise keep the last value and ask again later
getState() returns Failure(Timeout(afterSend = false))Time ran out before the request's write started or between retries, for example while the car answers busy during a wakeAsk again later; right after a wake pass a longer timeout
getState(DRIVE) returns Uncertain(ProtocolFault(RESPONSE_MTU_EXCEEDED))The reply is over the car's size limit; a long destination name makes this more likely. The car is not gone or asleepKeep the last value with its age and ask again later, not in a tight loop. There is no smaller query (16. Error Handling)
getState() returns Success, but a field is nullThe car did not send that field, or the reply had no vehicleDataShow "no value", never 0 (14, A8.3). Which fields a real car sends is not confirmed yet (#118)
getState() returns Success with the same values againSuccess is not proof of a new measurementCompare timestamp with the previous reply (14, A8.3)

Other messages

A command returns Uncertain with a timeout after a WARN (tag Dispatcher) Not signing a new copy: … may answer a possible duplicateA Bluetooth write reported failure, the library wrote the same bytes again, and the car answered that copy with an error, usually INVALID_TOKEN_OR_COUNTER (its counter was already used). The first copy may have run and its answer was lost, so the library sent no new copy and waited for that answer until the time limit (16, Retries and timeouts). The observation log shows the error on a [reply:vcsec] or [reply:infotainment] line with the same attempt=Read the state again; do not resend. If it keeps happening, check the link: writes are failing

Since 2026-11-07 such a request reports Uncertain(Timeout(afterSend = true)) once its write had started; older builds could report Failure(Timeout(afterSend = false)) for it (seen once on a real car, listKeys() in the background)Read the state again; do not resend. Nothing to repair. The late answer is not delivered (observation log)
